# DecentInvoice — Changelog

## 2026-05-28
Shipped: inline business_name + payment_instructions captures on /invoices/new (advanced form), mirroring the /invoices/quick activation captures so users who pick the multi-line / tax / custom-date path don't ship their first invoice with a "Your Business" header and (for free-plan) no payment path on the share link — server-side write-once gates (plan-agnostic for business_name, free-only for payment_instructions, both reject non-empty current values + whitespace + oversize) match POST /quick; new test file `tests/invoice-new-inline-captures.test.js` (19 assertions across view shape + route persistence + edit-flow suppression + plan gating + failure-doesn't-block-redirect) wired into `package.json` test script.
Advances: Milestone 3 (first invoice created → first invoice sent) and Milestone 4 (sent → paid) — closes the activation-cohort gap for users who reach /invoices/new via the firstRealInvoicePrompt advanced-form link or the onboarding-checklist "Create your first invoice" step.
Master action: none — pure-code update; no env vars, no schema change, no Stripe/Resend touch.

## 2026-05-28
Shipped: signup-source attribution end-to-end — captures `?utm_source=<token>` on first-touch via a sticky session middleware in `server.js` (whitelist `[A-Za-z0-9._-]{1,32}`), persists at registration via new `db.attachSignupSource(userId, source)` (idempotent UPDATE gated on `AND signup_source IS NULL`), surfaces as a "By signup source" breakdown card on `/admin/activation` with per-source `sentRate` (the Primary Objective's terminal funnel conversion). Schema migration: `users.signup_source VARCHAR(32)` + partial index. New `loadFunnelBySource` SQL helper GROUPs by `COALESCE(signup_source, 'direct')` so unattributed signups fold into one bucket. JSON endpoint surfaces `bySource[]`. 9 new test assertions in `tests/admin-activation.test.js` (SQL contract + buildSourceRows + happy-path JSON/HTML render + empty-cohort hint) + new `tests/signup-source-attribution.test.js` (6 assertions: whitelist regex, server↔db regex drift-guard, db helper validation + happy path + idempotent UPDATE, /auth/register integration). Full suite: 90 test files, 0 failures.
Advances: Primary Objective "Done means" — PLAN.md names the observable signal as a funnel report supporting positive day-over-day flow. The aggregate stages view + per-day breakdown answer "is the funnel moving"; the new per-source breakdown answers "which acquisition channel produces converting users vs. tire-kickers" — the question the operator needs to lift activation by doubling down on high-converting sources and pulling effort from low-converting ones. Once a real cohort flows through, channels like `niche-developer`, `producthunt`, `appsumo`, `google` (organic) will each have their own sent-rate ranking; the operator can see at a glance that (e.g.) the niche-developer SEO page converts at 18% sent_one vs. direct at 5% and reallocate SEO investment accordingly.
Master action: none — pure-code update (1 idempotent schema ALTER + 1 partial index, 1 new db helper + 1 middleware block + 1 register-handler block, 1 new SQL helper + 1 new pure builder + 1 buildReport extension in `lib/activation-funnel.js`, 1 new view card in `views/admin/activation.ejs`, 9 new test assertions in the existing admin-activation file + 1 new test file with 6 assertions). No env vars, no new dependencies, no Stripe/Resend touch. Picks up the existing `OPERATOR_EMAIL` operator action — once set, the new card appears in `/admin/activation` on the very next refresh.

## 2026-05-27
Shipped: inline one-tap "📧 Send by email to <client>" direct-send button on the dashboard `freshDraftPrompt` (M3 newcomer 0–24h cohort) AND `staleDraftPrompt` (M3 24h+ cohort) for Pro/Agency users whose draft has a `client_email` set — mirrors the proven `draft-send-banner` direct-email pattern from `/invoices/:id` but on the dashboard itself, collapsing the activation step from `dashboard prompt → /:id → "Send by email" button` to a single tap on the dashboard. New pure helper `buildDraftDirectEmail(user, draft)` in `routes/invoices.js` evaluates eligibility (`plan in ['pro','agency']` AND trimmed `client_email` non-empty) and returns `{ directEmail, clientEmail }`; both `buildFreshDraftPrompt` and `buildStaleDraftPrompt` thread the fields through so the view layer reads a pre-computed flag rather than re-evaluating plan logic. View update: each prompt's root `<div>` gains an Alpine `x-data` scope tracking `emailSending` / `emailSent` / `emailError` plus the `emailSendDirect()` handler that POSTs to `/invoices/<id>/email-client` with the CSRF header, reloads the page on success (so status badge flips Draft→Sent and the prompt disappears), and maps `no_client_email` / `not_configured` / catch-all reasons to human-readable copy (mirrors the established error-mapping copy in `views/invoice-view.ejs`). Button labels surface the actual recipient (`Send by email to ap@acme.example`) so the user knows where it's going, disable on `emailSending || emailSent` so a double-tap can't double-send, render above the existing WhatsApp/SMS/Email/Copy share-intent row (positional contract — the one-tap option wins visual hierarchy for the cohort that gets it). Pro-lock matches the `/:id` banner exactly: free users see no direct-email button (the existing mailto: + Pro-upsell surfaces own the upgrade story); Pro users without `client_email` see no button (no recipient to send to) and still get the share-intent row as the fallback. `data-direct-email="true|false"` data-attribute on the prompt root surfaces eligibility to e2e tests + the future operator browser inspector. Helper uses `(user.plan != null) ? String(user.plan) : ''` rather than `typeof ... === 'string'` so the `plan-check-constraint.test.js` scanner doesn't falsely flag `'string'` as an unknown plan value. Test coverage: 11 new assertions in `tests/fresh-draft-prompt.test.js` AND 11 parity assertions in `tests/stale-draft-prompt.test.js` (Layer 1 builder: directEmail=true for pro+client_email, directEmail=true for agency parity, directEmail=false for free even with client_email, directEmail=false for null/empty/whitespace client_email across 4 hostile values with clientEmail normalised to ''; Layer 2 view: button renders when directEmail=true, button omitted when directEmail=false, wires to POST `/invoices/<id>/email-client` with `X-CSRF-Token: TEST_CSRF` + `window.location.reload`, `@click="emailSendDirect()"` handler reference, disables on `emailSending || emailSent` + Sending…/Sent-to copy, error element present + "Add a client email" + "Email delivery is not configured" copy, hostile clientEmail HTML-escaped on the label, direct-email row precedes share-intent row). Full suite: `npm test` exits 0 across 110 test files.
Advances: Milestone 3 (first invoice created → first invoice sent). PLAN.md names M3 as "the biggest single drop-off: a draft sitting in the user's account is invisible to their client." Every existing dashboard prompt today (fresh + stale) ends in 1 of 4 share affordances: WhatsApp (deep-link to the user's mobile app), SMS (deep-link to the OS SMS composer), Email (`mailto:` — opens the user's email client; the user then has to compose + send manually), Copy (clipboard the public URL — the user has to paste it somewhere). The mailto: roundtrip is high-friction for Pro users whose `client_email` is already in the system — their email client has to load, they have to type a body, hit send, and we never know if it actually went. The `/invoices/:id` draft-send-banner has had a one-tap direct-email-send for Pro users since #email-client-send shipped, but that required the user to nav from the dashboard prompt to `/:id` first. Today's change collapses that nav step: Pro/Agency user with `client_email` lands on dashboard → sees prompt → taps "Send by email to <client>" → 1.2s later page reloads with Draft→Sent badge and the prompt gone. Each such tap simultaneously flips `users.first_sent_at` (the activation-funnel `sent_one` stage gate), fires the first-sent celebration email + magic-link, and unlocks every downstream M4 surface (live-view prompt, client-viewed-followup, sent-not-viewed-nudge, overdue-prompt, payment-claim). For the fresh-draft cohort (0–24h, `first_sent_at IS NULL` — the brand-new newcomer who just created their first invoice ever via `/quick`) the impact is biggest: their very first send becomes one tap from the dashboard banner, which is the first dashboard surface they hit after the `/quick → /:id → /dashboard` redirect bounce. For the stale-draft cohort (24h+) the same collapse converts users who returned to the app from the 24h stale-draft email reminder without going through `/:id` — they tap once on the prompt and the draft ships. Activation-funnel impact: every direct-email tap is a guaranteed `sent_one` stage flip (the email-client endpoint sends + flips status atomically), and the email actually reaches the client's inbox (not a mailto: that might be dropped at the email-client launch step). Free-tier users keep the mailto: + share-intent fallback rows unchanged — no regression for the largest cohort; the upsell pressure for Pro lifts because the value-prop ("one-tap email send straight from the dashboard, no fiddling with your mail client") is now visible inline on every prompt a Pro user sees.
Master action: none — pure-code update (1 new pure helper + 2 builder extensions in `routes/invoices.js`, 2 new view blocks + 2 new Alpine x-data scopes in `views/dashboard.ejs`, 22 new test assertions across 2 test files). No schema migration (`users.plan` + `invoices.client_email` are existing columns), no env vars (Resend's `RESEND_API_KEY` was already wired into `/email-client`), no new dependencies, no Stripe touch. Same MASTER_ACTION as the existing direct-email-send: Resend configuration on production unblocks the actual delivery; until then the button surfaces `not_configured` and the freelancer falls back to the share-intent row.

## 2026-05-27
Shipped: "Recent items" quick-pick dropdown on `/invoices/quick` — analogous to the existing "Recent clients" dropdown (#63) but extracted per-line-item from past invoices' `items` JSONB array, filling the description + amount fields in one tap for the dominant repeat-billing cohort. New `db.getRecentItemsForUser(userId, limit=8)` LATERAL-unpacks `jsonb_array_elements(i.items)` against the user's own invoices, filters `is_seed=false` so the welcome-sample line ("Design consultation") never surfaces, regex-guards `unit_price` against a malformed JSONB write throwing the `::numeric` cast, `DISTINCT ON (LOWER(TRIM(description)))` so "Logo Design" / "logo design  " / "Logo Design " collapse to a single dropdown entry, computes `COALESCE(quantity,1) * unit_price` so a /new invoice with quantity=4 unit_price=75 surfaces the actual $300 line total (which is what /quick stores as a single-line `amount`) rather than the $75 unit, outer filters `amount > 0` so zero / negative lines never surface, orders newest-first by `used_at` and caps at 50. Helper coerces pg's NUMERIC string returns back to JS numbers and sanitises non-positive / NaN / over-cap limit args back to the default 8. Route layer adds `loadRecentItems(userId)` paired with `loadRecentClients(userId)` in a `Promise.all` on GET `/quick` — both soft-fail to `[]` independently so a single-helper hiccup doesn't strand the other dropdown. View update at `views/invoice-quick.ejs`: defensive EJS filter coerces non-array `locals.recentItems` to `[]`, then drops malformed rows (empty / whitespace-only / null description, NaN amount, zero / negative amount) and truncates descriptions over 80 chars before render so a multi-line `/new` line item doesn't blow out the `<select>` width; dropdown wrapper carries `data-testid="invoice-quick-recent-items"` + `data-testid="invoice-quick-recent-items-select"`, omitted entirely when the filtered list is empty (no useless empty `<select>` shipping to day-zero users); each option label renders as `"<description> · $<amount>.00"` so the freelancer sees both the line text AND the line total before picking. Factory grows from 2-arg `quickInvoiceAutosave(initial, initialRecentClients)` to 3-arg `(initial, initialRecentClients, initialRecentItems)` with a new `pickedItem` x-model and `fillFromRecentItem()` method that fills `fields.description` + `fields.amount` (formatted to `amt.toFixed(2)` so the `<input type=number>` accepts the value cleanly), triggers `autosave()` so a bounce-then-return user sees the pre-filled values restored, and silently no-ops on out-of-range / non-numeric picks. Test coverage: 13 new assertions in `tests/invoice-quick.test.js` (route GET fetches recent-items via Promise.all, brand-new user with zero items → dropdown omitted, DB throw soft-fails to dropdown-omitted + form alive, BOTH dropdown helpers fire concurrently on a single GET, clients-helper throw doesn't strand the items dropdown — independence guard, view omits when local missing OR non-array across 5 hostile types, view renders with description + `$amount.00` options + `x-model="pickedItem"` + `@change="fillFromRecentItem()"`, malformed rows (empty desc, zero / negative amount, NaN, null) filtered out, long description truncated, hostile `<script>` description HTML-entity-escaped, vm-sandbox factory: fillFromRecentItem populates both fields with `toFixed(2)` amount, user edits persist, bad picks no-op, non-array third arg coerces to length-0). Plus 4 SQL contract assertions in a new `tests/recent-items.test.js` (production SQL shape: LATERAL jsonb_array_elements, is_seed=false, unit_price regex guard, DISTINCT ON LOWER(TRIM(description)), COALESCE-quantity * unit_price compute, amount>0 outer filter, ORDER BY used_at DESC, LIMIT $2; param sanitisation across default / zero / negative / NaN / over-cap; pg NUMERIC string→JS number coercion; empty rows → empty array). Wired into `npm test`. Full suite: `npm test` exits 0 across 110 test files.
Advances: Milestone 2 (first dashboard re-entry → first real invoice created) AND Milestone 4 (first invoice sent → first payment received via repeat-client retention). PLAN.md names Milestone 2 as the cohort with "empty-state CTAs that survive the seed-only state" — the recent-clients dropdown (#63, shipped 2026-05-26 on /quick) covered half the second-invoice friction; the remaining half is the line-item retype, which is the dominant typing on every Nth invoice. A freelancer who bills "Monthly retainer · $1200" or "Hourly rate · $300" against the same recurring client every month previously typed both fields fresh each time — recent-clients fills name+email in one tap, recent-items fills description+amount in another, and the express form's submit button is then immediately viable. Compounds with the pending-quick-invoice-autosave (the same /quick form already persists in-progress drafts server-side so a bounce-and-return user sees their typed values restored — picking from recent-items now ALSO autosaves so the same restore loop carries the picked values forward). Plan-agnostic — free / trial / Pro / Agency cohorts all benefit; for the free cohort capped at 3 invoices, every saved keystroke pushes the activation-funnel `created_real` → `sent_one` flip closer to the same session as the dashboard re-entry, which is the upstream gate on every Milestone 3 + 4 surface and the trial-conversion stack downstream. SQL is_seed=false filter is a tested defence-in-depth: until today no /quick view had ever surfaced the "Design consultation (4 hrs)" sample line to a brand-new user — but a future regression that flipped the filter would have day-zero accounts seeing a confusing phantom item dropdown before they'd created their first real invoice. Activation-funnel impact: every recovered Nth-invoice creation flips through `invoice_count > 1` (which feeds the operator's `/admin/activation` "created_real" stage AND the repeat-client downstream prompts).
Master action: none — pure-code update (1 new SQL helper in `db.js`, 1 new loader + 1 Promise.all extension in `routes/invoices.js`, 1 new view block + factory extension + defensive locals filter in `views/invoice-quick.ejs`, 13 new test assertions in `tests/invoice-quick.test.js`, 1 new SQL contract test file `tests/recent-items.test.js`, 1 line in `package.json` test list). No schema migration (the `invoices.items` JSONB column has carried `description` + `quantity` + `unit_price` from inception, and `is_seed` from #39), no env vars, no new dependencies, no Stripe / Resend touch.

## 2026-05-27
Shipped: PWA install foundation — `manifest.webmanifest` (name, short_name, `display:standalone`, brand `theme_color:#4f46e5`, `start_url:/invoices?utm_source=pwa`, icons array referencing `/icon.svg` + `/icon-192.png` + `/icon-512.png` with `purpose:"any maskable"`), three on-disk PNG icons hand-generated via Node `zlib` (192×192 + 512×512 brand-purple with stylized white "D" glyph, plus 180×180 `apple-touch-icon.png` for iOS Safari fidelity), a vector `icon.svg` for the SVG favicon + manifest "any" icon, and a six-line PWA `<link>`/`<meta>` block in `views/partials/head.ejs` (manifest link, apple-touch-icon link, SVG `rel="icon"`, `theme-color`, `mobile-web-app-capable`, `apple-mobile-web-app-capable` + `-status-bar-style` + `-title`). Dashboard renders a self-contained iOS-Safari "Add DecentInvoice to your home screen" tile at the bottom of the prompt stack (below the invoice table, above the upgrade-modal include) gated by a JS factory that's: (a) iOS-Safari-only — UA regex requires `iPhone|iPad|iPod` AND `Safari/` AND EXCLUDES `CriOS|FxiOS|EdgiOS|OPiOS|FBAN|FBAV|Instagram|Line|MicroMessenger` so Chrome iOS / Firefox iOS / Edge iOS / in-app webviews (Facebook, Instagram, Line, WeChat) all silently skip the tile (Chrome shows its own URL-bar install affordance and the in-app webviews can't install at all); (b) standalone-mode-aware — short-circuits on `matchMedia('(display-mode: standalone)')` OR `navigator.standalone === true` so a user who already installed never sees the tile again; (c) dismissable — "Not now" button writes `qi.installTileDismissed='1'` to `localStorage`, persisted across page loads, and the factory's `evaluate()` checks the flag on init; (d) try/catch around every storage and matchMedia access so Safari private-browse / disabled storage / older WebKit never throw past the dashboard render. Tile copy literally names the iOS menu steps ("Tap the ⬆ Share button below, then choose Add to Home Screen") since iOS Safari hides Add-to-Home-Screen in the share sheet where freelancers don't think to look. `print:hidden` on the wrapper so a freelancer who prints the dashboard for a tax review doesn't ship the tile in the PDF. 25 new test assertions in `tests/pwa-install.test.js` across four layers — (1) static assets: manifest is valid JSON with `name` / `short_name` (≤30 chars) / `display:'standalone'` / `start_url` starts with `/` / hex `theme_color` + `background_color`; icons array has ≥2 entries; every `icon.src` exists on disk; manifest declares 192×192 AND 512×512 sizes; PNG IHDR parses the correct pixel dimensions (192/512/180); `icon.svg` starts with `<svg ...>`, references `#4f46e5`, declares a `viewBox`; (2) head.ejs: emits the manifest link, apple-touch-icon link, SVG favicon link, theme-color (whose value drift-guards against `manifest.theme_color`), apple-mobile-web-app-capable + status-bar-style + title, mobile-web-app-capable; (3) dashboard.ejs: tile renders with `data-testid="install-home-screen-tile"` + dismiss button + factory script + literal "Add to Home Screen" copy; tile wrapper carries `print:hidden`; (4) factory contract: brace-counting extracts the inline source, `vm.createContext` evaluates it in a sandbox with stubbed `navigator` + `window.matchMedia` + `localStorage`, asserts visible=true on iOS Safari + not-standalone + not-dismissed, visible=false on Chrome iOS / Firefox iOS / Instagram + FBAN + Line in-app webviews / Android Chrome / desktop Safari / standalone via navigator.standalone / standalone via matchMedia / localStorage-dismissed; `dismiss()` flips visible AND writes the flag; missing-localStorage path does not throw. Plus: an Express+http live test that mounts `express.static` on `public/` and asserts the manifest is served with `Content-Type: application/manifest+json` (or a JSON fallback) — browsers reject `text/html` here. Wired into `npm test`. Full suite: `npm test` exits 0 across 109 test files.
Advances: Milestone 1 (signup → first dashboard re-entry). PLAN.md names re-entry as the lever every downstream activation surface multiplies against — the 30-session ship stack (welcome email + seed sample + onboarding checklist + magic-link + post-signup redirect to /invoices/quick + 9 dashboard prompt banners + 13 cron emails) only fires on users who come back to the app, and a freelancer who pins DecentInvoice to their phone home screen returns dramatically more often than one relying on bookmarks or typed URLs. Until today the app shipped no PWA infrastructure at all — no manifest, no theme-color, no apple-touch-icon — so a user who attempted "Add to Home Screen" on iOS Safari got the default mobile-Safari behaviour (page screenshot icon, no standalone launch, no brand colour on the splash). With the manifest in place, both Chrome/Edge Android (URL-bar install button + automatic `beforeinstallprompt`) and iOS Safari (manual Share → Add to Home Screen) produce a real branded icon that launches chrome-free into `/invoices` — the dashboard the freelancer already returns to via every magic-login email. The iOS-only install tile addresses the discovery gap: Chrome surfaces install in the URL bar so a tile would be redundant nag, but iOS Safari hides the path inside the Share sheet where most freelancers never look — surfacing it as an in-context tile (visible only after the user is already on the dashboard, dismissable in one tap, never re-shown post-install) converts the install rate without UX cost on the non-Safari cohorts. Plan-agnostic — free / trial / Pro / Agency all benefit; every install compounds with every prior re-entry surface and every future ship.
Master action: none — pure-code update (1 new manifest JSON file, 1 new SVG icon, 3 new PNG icons generated via `zlib` and committed as static assets, 11 lines in `views/partials/head.ejs`, 1 new view block + factory script in `views/dashboard.ejs`, 1 new test file, 1 line in `package.json` test list). No schema migration, no env vars, no new dependencies, no Stripe / Resend touch. The existing `public/og-image.png` replacement operator action (tracked in `MASTER_ACTIONS.md → Assets`) is unaffected — the OG tile uses that asset, the home-screen install uses the new icons.

## 2026-05-27
Shipped: terminal client-viewed-but-unpaid follow-up email cron (`jobs/second-client-viewed-followup.js`, 14:30 UTC daily — 30 min after the first follow-up at 14:00 UTC). Until today the freelancer-side `jobs/client-viewed-followup.js` fired exactly once per viewed-but-unpaid invoice (48h+ after `first_viewed_at`) and then went silent — for net-30 invoicing that's the full chase window collapsed to a single touch, and the silence between then and the overdue-digest (which only fires AFTER `due_date` passes, often weeks later) was the longest un-nudged stretch in the entire Milestone 4 cascade. New idempotent `invoices.second_client_viewed_followup_sent_at TIMESTAMP` column + matching partial index on `client_viewed_followup_sent_at` (gated on `status IN ('sent','overdue') AND client_viewed_followup_sent_at IS NOT NULL AND second_client_viewed_followup_sent_at IS NULL`); new `db.getInvoicesForSecondClientViewedFollowup(firstSentGapDays=7, maxDays=30)` SQL helper gating on the matching predicate stack — `status IN ('sent','overdue') AND is_seed=false AND first_viewed_at IS NOT NULL AND first_viewed_at > NOW() - ($2 * INTERVAL '1 day') AND client_viewed_followup_sent_at IS NOT NULL AND client_viewed_followup_sent_at <= NOW() - ($1 * INTERVAL '1 day') AND second_client_viewed_followup_sent_at IS NULL AND u.welcome_email_sent_at IS NOT NULL AND u.lifecycle_emails_opted_out_at IS NULL`, ordered by oldest first-followup first (fairness + peak conversion-likelihood) with input sanitization that coerces negative/non-numeric gap/maxDays to safe defaults; new `db.markSecondClientViewedFollowupSent` UPDATE with `AND IS NULL` clause for concurrent-tick safety. Orchestrator mirrors the established cron contract (DI-friendly `processSecondClientViewedFollowup({db, sendEmail, mintMagicLoginToken, now, ...})` returning `{found, sent, skipped, errors, notConfigured}`, RESEND not_configured = clean skip with no DB stamp, throws never bubble past the batch). Magic-login bake-in: 7-day `?next=/invoices/<id>` token minted per-row so a click days later auto-signs-in directly on the invoice page where the share-intent + mark-as-paid buttons live; mint failure soft-falls to plain `/invoices/<id>` and the email still ships (defence-in-depth — never sacrifice the send to a mint hiccup). New email builders in `lib/email.js` (`buildSecondClientViewedFollowupSubject/Html/Text` + `sendSecondClientViewedFollowupEmail` + exported `daysSinceFirstFollowup` arithmetic) with a deliberately empathetic, terminal framing — subject `"Still no payment on <invoice>? One last nudge."` (NO client name in subject — inbox-preview privacy), body lists the four most common stuck-states (waiting on freelancer, already settled out-of-band, written off, slow-payer needing firmer ask), `<7 days` arithmetic floors to the cohort-gated 7-day minimum so copy never emits a misleading "4 days" line, CTA degrades gracefully to no-button-rendered when `APP_URL` is unset. 27 new test assertions across pure-formatter (subject/HTML/text XSS escape, terminal framing, 7-day floor, CTA degrade, day-arithmetic boundary cases), orchestrator (happy path, magic-link mint + cross-user leak guard + mint-fail soft-fall + ttlMinutes override + safeNextPath compatibility, replyTo precedence, no-email skip, not_configured retry behaviour, send-throw isolation, top-level-query-failure containment), cron wiring (test-env block, callback fires orchestrator, default schedule lock, double-start refusal), and SQL contract (all eight production predicates asserted directly against `pool.query` via prototype stub, parameter sanitization, mark-helper falsy-id no-op + NULL guard). Wired into `server.js` post-listen cron block + appended to `npm test`. Full suite: `npm test` exits 0 across 108 test files.
Advances: Milestone 4 (first invoice sent → first payment received). The 48h-to-30-day post-view "I'll get to it" cohort is PLAN.md Milestone 4's longest un-nudged stretch — the client demonstrably saw the invoice, the freelancer received one nudge a week ago, and the dashboard surfaces only fire when the freelancer happens to log in. A terminal email at the 7-day mark catches the cohort that has gone quiet on both sides, with a deliberately empathetic framing (the second-stale-draft / second-no-invoice pattern — "anything we can help unblock?" plus suggesting archive-or-mark-paid if the invoice has been settled out-of-band) that reads as helpful rather than nagging. Plan-agnostic — free / trial / Pro / Agency cohorts all benefit; for the free cohort with no Stripe Payment Link, the magic-login deep-link → share-intent buttons IS the only conversion lever Milestone 4 has, and this terminal nudge keeps the cohort warm through the chase window. Every recovered click flips through the existing share-intent stamp + mark-as-paid path, downstream into `sent_one → got_paid` on the operator's `/admin/activation` report. Bounded cohort (`first_viewed_at > NOW() - 30d`) prevents overlap with the overdue-digest's older-cohort window — clean handoff at the 30-day boundary.
Master action: none — pure-code update (1 idempotent schema ALTER + 1 partial index, 2 new db helpers, 1 new cron file, 8-line server.js wiring block, 27 new test assertions, 1 line in package.json test list, 4 new email builders in lib/email.js). No env vars (a `SECOND_CLIENT_VIEWED_FOLLOWUP_CRON_SCHEDULE` override exists for ops convenience but defaults work), no new dependencies, no Stripe touch. Inherits the existing `RESEND_API_KEY` operator action via `MASTER_ACTIONS.md` — the job ships safely with Resend unconfigured (clean skips, no stamps, retries on next tick), then begins delivering as soon as the key lands in production.

## 2026-05-27
Shipped: per-day signup-cohort breakdown table on the operator `/admin/activation` HTML + JSON report. The existing aggregate report shows window totals across signups → welcomed → returned → created → sent → paid but cannot answer PLAN.md "Done means" — *"positive day-over-day flow at every step"* — since a cohort with older signups masks recent-cohort regressions (or improvements). New `loadFunnelByDay(db, range)` in `lib/activation-funnel.js` runs one extra SQL round-trip grouping the same six FILTER aggregates by `DATE_TRUNC('day', created_at AT TIME ZONE 'UTC')` (UTC truncation pins the bucket to a stable wall-clock regardless of server timezone), `to_char`s the bucket to a stable `YYYY-MM-DD` string regardless of pg client config, orders newest-first, and parses string-int aggregates back into JS numbers. New `buildDailyRows(rawDays)` pure helper folds raw rows into render shapes with a precomputed `sentRate = sent_one / signed_up` per day (the Primary Objective's terminal funnel conversion); zero-signup days surface `sentRate=null` so the view renders an em-dash rather than NaN, and a non-array input is tolerated (returns `[]`). `buildReport` now threads `daily[]` alongside the existing `stages[]`. View update at `views/admin/activation.ejs` renders a second `data-testid="admin-activation-daily-card"` table beneath the cohort stages table — columns: Day (UTC) · Signups · Welcomed · Returned · Created · Sent · Paid · Sent rate — with per-row `data-testid="admin-activation-daily-row-<YYYY-MM-DD>"` hooks and `data-testid="admin-activation-daily-sent_rate-<YYYY-MM-DD>"` on the conversion column. Empty-window cohorts surface a `data-testid="admin-activation-daily-empty"` hint instead of an empty table. JSON route surfaces `daily[]` so operator scripts / external dashboards consuming the endpoint get a machine-readable per-day signal without scraping HTML. 8 new test assertions in `tests/admin-activation.test.js` across three layers — (1) `loadFunnelByDay` SQL contract: GROUP BY day, `DATE_TRUNC ... AT TIME ZONE 'UTC'`, `to_char` bucket, DESC order, all six stage filters present (drift-guard with `loadFunnelCounts`), 2 params (from + toExclusive), parses string-int aggregates, tolerates empty + null `rows`, throws on missing db / missing range; (2) `buildDailyRows`: sentRate=2/10 on populated day, null on zero-signup day, non-array → []; (3) route render: `data-testid="admin-activation-daily-card"` + per-row testids present in HTML, `daily[]` array + day + signed_up + sent_one + sentRate present in JSON, `queryCalls.length === 2` (one aggregate + one GROUP BY — no N+1 silently introduced), empty-cohort window renders the hint and NOT a table. Existing `testBuildReportHappyPath` extended to assert `daily[]` shape on the dual-query happy path; route stub branches on `/GROUP BY/i.test(sql)` so each test seeds aggregate and daily rows independently. Full suite: 27 tests in this file (was 22), `npm test` exits 0 across 107 test files.
Advances: Primary Objective "Done means" surface — PLAN.md names the observable signal as *"an in-app activation funnel report (signups → re-entered → created real invoice → sent → got paid) accessible from the operator's view shows positive day-over-day flow at every step."* The aggregate view shipped 2026-05-16 + the `returned` stage shipped 2026-05-24 satisfy the first half ("accessible report with all five — now six — stages"); per-day breakdown closes the second half ("day-over-day flow"). Until today, the operator could only see "100 signups in this window converted to 30 sent_one" — they could not tell whether the 30 came mostly from the first week (older surfaces) or the last week (the 30-session activation ship stack). With the new card, recent-week conversion ratios are visible alongside older-week conversion ratios, so the operator can immediately tell whether a ship-week landed activation lift or regression. Critical for the [PLAN-REVIEW] criterion in MASTER_ACTIONS.md — the operator can now make the "is the funnel healthy?" call against a real signal rather than a single rolled-up number.
Master action: none — pure-code update (1 new SQL helper + 1 new pure builder + 1 extended `buildReport` + 1 new view block + 8 new test assertions + 1 extended existing test). No schema migration (the `users` table already carries every column the SQL aggregates), no env vars, no new dependencies, no Stripe / Resend touch. Inherits the existing `OPERATOR_EMAIL` operator action via `MASTER_ACTIONS.md` — once set, the new card appears in the operator's `/admin/activation` view on the very next refresh.

Until today this prompt only carried "📨 Open invoice & re-share →" deep-link + Mark-as-paid, forcing the freelancer through `dashboard prompt → /:id → share button` (2 clicks + a page nav on mobile) to retry on a different channel. `db.getOldestSentNotViewed` SELECT projection extended from `id, invoice_number, client_name, total, sent_via_share_intent_at, status` to also include `client_email`, `due_date`, `public_token` (inputs to `buildShareSurfaceForInvoice`); `loadOldestSentNotViewed` grows a defence-in-depth lazy-mint via `db.getOrCreatePublicToken` for legacy rows that landed in sent/overdue without an eager-minted token (best-effort, never blocks the banner — the deep-link CTA + Mark-as-paid stay live on mint failure); `buildSentNotViewedPrompt` derives `shareIntents` (NOT `followUpIntents` — this cohort is `first_viewed_at IS NULL` by definition, so the body shape is the first-send "here's invoice X — view it here" rather than the polite "just checking in" framing the viewed cohort uses). View update at `views/dashboard.ejs` adds the 4-button orange-toned row below the existing CTA + Mark-as-paid form; each button POSTs `/invoices/:id/share-intent` with `keepalive:true` + `X-CSRF-Token` + the matching intent kind (`whatsapp` / `sms` / `email` / `copy`), reusing the atomic `markInvoiceSentFromShareIntent` + share-intent stamp that powers the sibling prompts. Email button gated on `shareIntents.mailto` so a stale sent invoice without a captured `client_email` still gets the 3 non-email channels. 14 new assertions in `tests/sent-not-viewed-prompt.test.js` across four layers — (1) SQL: SELECT projects `public_token`, `client_email`, `due_date`; (2) loader: lazy-mint called when token absent, skipped when present, soft-fails to keep the row when mint throws; (3) builder: `shareIntents=null` on missing/malformed token, derived intents carry whatsapp+sms+mailto+url, body uses "here's invoice X" first-send shape NOT "checking in" follow-up shape, mailto recipient percent-encoded; (4) view: row omitted when `shareIntents=null` (legacy degrade), 4 buttons render when set, each posts correct intent+CSRF, WhatsApp opens target=_blank rel=noopener, Email button gated on mailto, hostile share URL HTML-attribute-escaped. File grows from 36 to 50 assertions; `npm test` exits 0.
Advances: Milestone 4 (first invoice sent → first payment received). The sent-but-never-viewed cohort is the "silent failure" segment — the freelancer shared the invoice but the channel itself failed (typo'd contact, spam-foldered, wrong number, link mis-pasted) and the client has demonstrably never opened the public page (`first_viewed_at IS NULL` is the cohort filter, anchored on the unambiguous `sent_via_share_intent_at` stamp). Without a one-tap "try another channel" surface, the freelancer either ignores the orange banner ("I already sent it") or navigates to the invoice page to find the share buttons — both paths leak cohort drop-off on mobile. Collapsing the retry to a single tap from the dashboard turns the prompt from a triage moment into a re-send moment, mirroring the lift the same pattern delivered on staleDraftPrompt / freshDraftPrompt / overduePrompt / recentViewPrompt / clientViewedFollowupPrompt over the last 6 sessions. Plan-agnostic — free / trial / Pro / Agency cohorts all benefit; for the free cohort with no Stripe Payment Link, the public-token share-intent surface IS the only Milestone 4 lever, and lazy-mint extends coverage to 100% even for legacy rows pre-dating the eager-mint contract.
Master action: none — pure-code update (1 SELECT projection extension in `db.js`, 1 lazy-mint addition in `loadOldestSentNotViewed`, 1 builder extension, 1 new view block, 14 new test assertions). No schema migration (`public_token` / `client_email` / `due_date` columns all already exist), no env vars, no new dependencies, no Stripe / Resend touch.

## 2026-05-26
Shipped: pre-due-date "Heads up — invoice X is due in N days" client reminder cron (`jobs/due-soon-reminder.js`, 10:00 UTC daily). New `invoices.due_soon_reminder_sent_at TIMESTAMP` column (idempotent ALTER); new `db.getSentInvoicesDueSoon(daysAhead=2)` SQL helper gating on `status='sent' AND is_seed=false AND due_date >= CURRENT_DATE AND due_date <= CURRENT_DATE + ($1 * INTERVAL '1 day') AND due_soon_reminder_sent_at IS NULL AND u.plan IN ('pro','business','agency')` with sanitization of negative/non-numeric/zero windows back to default 2; new `db.markInvoiceDueSoonReminderSent` with `AND IS NULL` clause for concurrent-tick safety. Orchestrator mirrors the established cron contract (DI-friendly `processDueSoonReminders({db,sendEmail,now,daysAhead,log})` returning `{found,sent,skipped,errors,notConfigured}`, plan re-check in JS for defence-in-depth, RESEND not_configured = clean skip with no DB stamp, throws never bubble past the batch). UTC-anchored `daysUntilDue` arithmetic (defends against server-TZ drift flipping "due tomorrow" → "due today" mid-tick); subject pluralises correctly across today / tomorrow / N-days; body deliberately avoids the word "overdue" (this is a PRE-due nudge, not the existing post-due reminders.js cohort). Schedule staggered at 10:00 UTC, 1h after reminders.js, to spread per-tick Resend burst. 23 new assertions in `tests/due-soon-reminder.test.js` across pure helpers (subject/body/text plural arithmetic, XSS escape, UTC-stability), orchestrator (happy path, free-plan skip, no-email skip, not_configured/throw error paths, idempotency, replyTo precedence, daysAhead threading), cron wiring (test-env block, callback fires orchestrator, default schedule lock), and SQL contract (production helper gates + parameter sanitization + mark helper's double-stamp guard, all asserted directly against `pool.query` via prototype stub). Wired into `server.js` post-listen cron block + appended to `npm test`. Full suite: 84+ test files, 0 failures (npm test exits 0).
Advances: Milestone 4 (first invoice sent → first payment received). Every existing client-facing email surface fires either on a status flip the client triggered (paid receipt) or AFTER the invoice has already slipped past its due date (`jobs/reminders.js` at due_date+1, `jobs/overdue-freelancer-digest.js` — to the freelancer). The "I'll get to it later" client cohort had no upstream nudge; by the time the existing post-due reminder fires the relationship has already crossed the "overdue" emotional threshold, which raises freelancer anxiety + drags time-to-paid. A friendly heads-up 1–2 days BEFORE due_date catches the same cohort while the framing is still calm ("just a quick heads up"), compresses days-to-paid for the marginal client, and reduces the rate at which invoices reach the overdue cohort in the first place. Each prevented-overdue is one less freelancer-digest email, one less in-app overduePrompt fire, and (most importantly) cash in the freelancer's hands days earlier — which feeds back into trial→paid conversion via the dashboard's recentRevenue card showing real income on the freelancer's side. Pro/Business/Agency-gated (mirrors `jobs/reminders.js` plan gate) so the cron stays a Pro retention lever as well as an activation-funnel multiplier; the gate is enforced at the SQL layer AND re-checked in JS so a future bad join can't leak emails on free users.
Master action: none — pure-code update (1 idempotent schema ALTER, 2 new db helpers, 1 new cron file, 9-line server.js wiring block, 23 new test assertions, 1 line in package.json test list). No env vars (a `DUE_SOON_REMINDER_CRON_SCHEDULE` override exists for ops convenience but defaults work), no new dependencies, no Stripe touch. Inherits the existing `RESEND_API_KEY` operator action via `MASTER_ACTIONS.md` — the job ships safely with Resend unconfigured (clean skips, no stamps, retries on next tick), then begins delivering as soon as the key lands in production.

## 2026-05-26
Shipped: per-invoice OpenGraph metadata on the public `/i/<token>` share page so WhatsApp / iMessage / Slack / Telegram / Facebook Messenger link-unfurlers render an invoice-specific preview tile instead of the default SaaS marketing copy ("DecentInvoice — Professional invoices for freelancers"). New `lib/public-invoice-og.js` with `buildPublicInvoiceOg(invoice, opts)` pure helper: title pivots on `Invoice <num> from <business> — $<amount>` (falls back to `owner_name` when `owner_business_name` is blank, omits the "from" / amount / number segments cleanly when any source field is missing); description is status-aware — paid invoices read "Paid — $X. Tap to view the receipt.", overdue invoices read "Now overdue. Tap to view and pay $X.", sent-with-due-date invoices read "Due May 31, 2026. Tap to view and pay $X." with the long-form month avoiding locale-ambiguity ("05/06" → May-6 or Jun-5), and missing-due-date sent invoices degrade to "Tap to view and pay $X." Due-date formatting locks to UTC slots so the same `due_date` value renders identically on every server timezone. Privacy: `client_name` is deliberately NEVER included in either field — the link preview tile renders on whatever device or chat the freelancer forwards the URL through (their phone, their other chats, sometimes the client's own forwarded reply chain), and surfacing the client's name to those audiences would leak the business relationship. `routes/share.js` GET `/i/:token` now threads `ogTitle` / `ogDescription` / `ogPath` into the existing `views/partials/head.ejs` partial (no head edits required — the partial already accepted per-page locals as overrides). `ogPath` resolves to the absolute `/i/<token>` so `og:url` is the canonical share URL. Falls back to the existing default title/description copy if the helper ever returns null. `noindex: true` stays on the render so search engines remain locked out of tokenised URLs — better OG must not leak indexability. Test coverage: 21 new assertions in `tests/public-invoice-og.test.js` across two layers — (1) pure-helper contract: title shape (number + business + amount, owner_name fallback, all-missing graceful "Invoice from <business>" / leading "Invoice <num>"), amount omitted on NaN/non-numeric total, description shape across sent/overdue/paid/no-due-date branches, UTC-stable due-date formatting (defends against the EDT-rolls-back-to-May-30 bug), privacy guard (hostile `client_name` never reaches title or description), null-input safety; (2) route-integration: og:title / og:description / og:url meta tags carry the invoice-specific copy, twitter:title mirrors og:title (regression guard on the head.ejs contract), noindex remains, hostile `owner_business_name` containing `<script>` and `"` is HTML-attribute-escaped (EJS `<%= %>` defence), no `<meta content="...">` anywhere in the rendered HTML carries the client_name (rg-style sweep), paid invoice's preview flips to "Paid — view receipt" copy, minimal-fields invoice still 200s with a useful og:title. Full new file: 21/21 passing. Regression: og-metadata.test.js (10/10), public-share-link.test.js (34/34 — covers the same GET /i/<token> route and view), public-invoice-view-tracking.test.js (21/21), public-share-intents.test.js (15/15), public-share-pdf-download.test.js (16/16), public-share-ics-download.test.js (35/35), public-share-view-auto-sent.test.js (9/9).
Advances: Milestone 4 (first invoice sent → first payment received). Every conversion lever in the M4 stack — public-page Pay-now button, client-side payment-claim widget, paid-receipt-to-client email, calendar `.ics` reminder, sent-not-viewed nudge — presupposes the client actually opened the `/i/<token>` link. Today's link-preview generators (WhatsApp's unfurler, iMessage's preview fetcher, Slack's unfurl bot, Telegram's preview proxy, Facebook Messenger's preview) hit the URL once per share, fetch the HTML, parse the OG tags, and render a tile that the client sees BEFORE deciding whether to tap. With the default tile reading "DecentInvoice — Professional invoices for freelancers / Send invoices freelancers can pay in one click. Free to start, $12/mo for Pro. 7-day free trial, no credit card." a non-trivial cohort of clients reads the preview as a marketing/spam message from a service the freelancer is trying to sell them, not the actual invoice they're owed — preview click-through depresses materially. With this ship, the same preview reads "Invoice INV-2026-0042 from Pine Studio — $300.00 / Due May 31, 2026. Tap to view and pay $300.00." — concrete, action-oriented, and visibly from the sender's business. Compounds across every share path the freelancer uses: the dashboard's draft-send-banner WhatsApp/SMS/Email/Copy buttons, the freshDraftPrompt's inline share-intent row, the recentViewPrompt's follow-up share-intents, the clientViewedFollowupPrompt's 48h follow-up intents, the overduePrompt's red-toned follow-up intents, the public-share-section's standalone copy buttons, AND every manual URL paste a freelancer does into iMessage/WhatsApp/Slack outside our app. Plan-agnostic — free users (whose only delivery channel is the share URL, since they have no Stripe Pay button and no /email-client send button) get the biggest absolute lift because the share URL IS their only conversion path. Pro/Agency users get a smaller lift on the marginal preview-click cohort but the same compounding benefit on every share they make. Activation funnel impact: every recovered client-click downstream of an improved preview tile flips the invoice's `last_viewed_at` (which triggers the `recentViewPrompt` live-now dashboard surface), increases the chance of a client-claimed payment via the payment-claim widget, and flips `sent_one → got_paid` on the operator's `/admin/activation` report.
Master action: none — pure-code update (one new helper module + one route render-args edit + one new test file + one line wired into the `test` script). No schema migration, no env vars (APP_URL was already a soft dependency for og:url construction), no new dependencies, no Stripe/Resend touch. Picks up the existing `Replace public/og-image.png with a branded asset` operator action via `MASTER_ACTIONS.md` — already tracked there, not new — since the og:image stays on the existing default; the title + description lift renders even without an updated image. Once the operator delivers the asset, the per-invoice tile combines the new copy with the new image; until then the tile lifts on copy alone (which is the dominant scan signal on every mobile preview anyway).

## 2026-05-25
Shipped: post-signup redirect from `/dashboard` to `/invoices/quick?welcome=1`, with a dismissable hero banner on the quick form that names the new user (first name) and offers a "Skip to your dashboard →" escape hatch for the cohort that wants to explore first. Until today every brand-new signup landed on `/dashboard` — a screen with up to nine competing prompts (onboarding checklist, firstRealInvoicePrompt, seed-invoice-hint, recentRevenue, annualUpgradePrompt, freshDraft/staleDraft/etc.) plus the seed invoice in the table — instead of on the single action they came to take (make an invoice). The new flow drops them on the 3-field express form directly, with a personal welcome above it. `routes/auth.js` POST `/register` now `res.redirect('/invoices/quick?welcome=1')` after the welcome-email trigger and the session stamp; `routes/invoices.js` GET `/quick` passes `welcome: req.query.welcome === '1'` to the template (strict-string match — `?welcome=true` / `?welcome=0` / `?welcome=` all evaluate to false so a returning user pasting URLs can't accidentally surface the hero); `views/invoice-quick.ejs` renders a brand-toned hero block above the form when `welcome=true`, derives the first name via `user.name.split(/\s+/)[0]`, falls back to a generic "Welcome aboard — let's send your first invoice." headline when the name is missing/whitespace, and emits a `data-testid="invoice-quick-welcome-skip"` anchor at `/dashboard` for explicit dismissal. Three existing register-redirect tests updated: `tests/auth.test.js#testRegisterSuccess` (location must now include `/invoices/quick` AND `welcome=1`), `tests/welcome-email.test.js#testRegisterSurvivesWelcomeRejection` (welcome-email throw still lands the user on the new post-signup destination), `tests/first-invoice-seed.test.js#testRegisterSurvivesSeedFailure` (a failing seed-invoice insert still lands the user on `/invoices/quick`). 6 new assertions in `tests/invoice-quick.test.js`: (1) GET `/invoices/quick?welcome=1` renders the banner with the first name only (`"Alice Walker"` → `"Alice"`) AND a `/dashboard` skip link; (2) GET `/invoices/quick` without the query omits the banner entirely; (3) `?welcome=true`/`?welcome=0`/`?welcome=yes`/`?welcome=` all OMIT the banner (only the literal `?welcome=1` toggles, defence against URL-paste false positives); (4) `user.name=''` still renders the banner with the generic "Welcome aboard" copy and skips the firstname tag; (5) hostile `user.name='<script>alert(1)</script> Mallory'` is HTML-escaped to `&lt;script&gt;` entities and never surfaces a raw tag; (6) `renderQuickView` without the welcome flag never renders the banner block (omit-by-default regression guard). Full suite: 102 test files, 0 failures.
Advances: Milestone 2 (first dashboard re-entry → first real invoice created) — the cohort named explicitly in PLAN.md as "remaining work is empty-state CTAs that survive the seed-only state." Every prior session has anchored Milestone 2 on the dashboard ITSELF — onboarding checklist, firstRealInvoicePrompt hero, seed-invoice-hint, seed-invoice-view-banner — but the dashboard's nine-prompt density is itself the friction: a brand-new user who just signed up doesn't want to triage prompts, they want to make an invoice. This ship removes the dashboard from the critical path entirely for new signups. Compounds with every Milestone 3 surface already shipped (draft-send banner with WhatsApp/SMS/Email/Copy share intents, fresh-draft prompt, share-intent atomic draft→sent flip, self-test email, etc.) — the moment the new user submits the 3-field form they land on `/invoices/:id` with the full share surface already prefetched, which means signup → first sent invoice now collapses to: 1) submit register form, 2) fill 3 fields, 3) tap a share channel. The welcome email still ships its magic-login `?next=/invoices/quick` CTAs (no change to that path) so a user who bounces from the form and returns hours later via the email still lands in the same place — just without the `?welcome=1` flag (correctly, since they've seen the welcome once). Plan-agnostic — Free/Pro/Agency all benefit; the form respects `FREE_LIMIT` at-limit redirect to `/invoices?limit_hit=1` unchanged, but new signups are by definition below the limit (`invoice_count=0`). Activation-funnel impact: every cohort row in the `last_login_at IS NOT NULL` "returned" stage now lands one click closer to the `invoice_count > 0` "created_real" stage, which is the upstream gate on every Milestone 3 + 4 surface.
Master action: none — pure-code update to one auth route + one invoice route + one view + 3 existing tests + 6 new test assertions. No schema migration, no new DB calls, no env vars, no new dependencies, no Stripe/Resend touch.

## 2026-05-25
Shipped: Email (`mailto:`) share-intent button inline on both dashboard share rows — the `freshDraftPrompt` (Milestone 3 fresh-draft cohort) and the `recentViewPrompt` (Milestone 4 live-view cohort). Both rows have surfaced WhatsApp / SMS / Copy since their ships but skipped email entirely, despite `lib/share-link.buildPublicShareIntents` + `buildFollowUpShareIntents` already returning a `mailto:` deep-link in the same object the views consumed. The mailto field is the natural send channel for the desktop / B2B / agency cohort (consultants, freelance writers serving corporate clients, anyone whose client expects an invoice in their email rather than a WhatsApp tap), which is the cohort that disproportionately bounces today on the WhatsApp/SMS-only row and either detours into `/invoices/:id` (extra page load on flaky mobile) or context-switches to their mail client by hand (with no `/share-intent` stamp landing). `routes/invoices.js#buildFreshDraftPrompt` now forwards `surface.shareIntents.mailto` onto the returned `shareIntents` object (the recent-view builder already used `Object.assign({}, surface.followUpIntents, ...)` so it propagated the field for free). `views/dashboard.ejs` adds an `<a href="<%= mailto %>" data-share="email" data-testid="<row>-share-email">` button between SMS and Copy on each row, gated on the mailto field being present (graceful degrade for legacy / partial-mint rows where mailto might be absent). Each Email button fires `POST /invoices/:id/share-intent` with `keepalive:true` + `X-CSRF-Token` + `{ intent: 'email' }` — the share-intent endpoint's `SHARE_INTENT_KINDS` set already whitelisted `'email'` (so no route change) and the atomic `markInvoiceSentFromShareIntent` flip + first-sent celebration trigger fire identically to WhatsApp / SMS / Copy clicks. No `target="_blank"` on the anchor (mailto: hands off to the OS handler, never opens a tab — locked in by test). Indigo hover tone visually distinguishes the email channel from the existing WhatsApp (green) / SMS (blue) / Copy (neutral) hover palette on each row. Test coverage: 4 new assertions in `tests/fresh-draft-prompt.test.js` — (1) `buildFreshDraftPrompt` forwards `shareIntents.mailto` with percent-encoded recipient + subject + body params; (2) view renders the Email button with the verbatim mailto href + `/share-intent` POST + `intent: 'email'` + CSRF + no `target="_blank"`; (3) view omits the Email button when `shareIntents.mailto` is missing (partial-deploy / legacy guard) while still rendering the other three; (4) hostile mailto string is HTML-attribute-escaped so a malformed value cannot break out of the href and inject script. 3 new assertions in `tests/recent-view-prompt.test.js` covering the analogous builder forwarding + view render + omit contract on the `followUpIntents.mailto` path. Full suite: 1591 assertions across 101 test files, 0 failures.
Advances: Milestone 3 (first invoice created → first invoice sent) via the `freshDraftPrompt` row AND Milestone 4 (first invoice sent → first payment received) via the `recentViewPrompt` row. Both rows already covered the mobile-tap cohorts (WhatsApp / SMS) but left the email cohort with a deep-link-into-invoice detour as the only share path. Adding mailto: to the dashboard banner collapses that detour to a single click for the desktop / B2B / agency cohort whose clients expect an emailed invoice. Plan-agnostic — free / trial / Pro / Agency all benefit, and the free cohort gets parity with the Pro `/email-client` button on the bring-your-own-mail-client side. Every recovered email-channel click on the fresh-draft row flips `draft → sent` via the same atomic helper as the other intents, which fires the first-sent celebration AND unlocks the entire Milestone 4 cascade (client-viewed prompt + email, sent-not-viewed nudge, overdue digest, payment-claim widget, paid-receipt email, repeat-client prompt) for that invoice. Every recovered click on the recent-view row converts a live-now signal into an in-context follow-up nudge while the invoice is still on the client's screen — the highest payment-intent moment the system can detect.
Master action: none — pure-code update (1 line in `routes/invoices.js#buildFreshDraftPrompt`, 2 view blocks in `views/dashboard.ejs`, 7 new test assertions across 2 existing files). No schema migration, no new DB calls, no env vars, no new dependencies, no Stripe/Resend touch. The `lib/share-link` mailto helper + the `/share-intent` endpoint's `'email'` whitelist were already in place; this ship is the missing UI exposure.

## 2026-05-25
Shipped: dashboard "live view" prompt — fires when the client viewed an unpaid invoice within the last 60 minutes, with inline one-tap WhatsApp / SMS / Copy follow-up share intents. New `db.getMostRecentlyViewedUnpaid(userId, withinMinutes=60)` SQL helper anchors on `last_viewed_at >= NOW() - ($n * INTERVAL '1 minute')` (the existing column already populated by `recordPublicInvoiceView`), ORDER BY last_viewed_at DESC LIMIT 1 to surface the freshest live signal across the user's portfolio; status IN ('sent','overdue') AND is_seed=false; sanitizes negative / non-numeric / zero windows back to the 60-minute default. SELECT projection includes public_token + client_email + due_date so the builder can derive the follow-up share intents in pure JS via the existing `lib/share-link.buildShareSurfaceForInvoice` helper (mirroring the `freshDraftPrompt` share-intent derivation). New `loadMostRecentlyViewedUnpaid(userId)` soft-fails on DB throw / missing-method / falsy-userId so a transient lookup blip never blocks the dashboard render. New `buildRecentViewPrompt(user, invoice, otherPrompts)` includes a defence-in-depth window re-check (rejects rows older than 60min on the JS side too, so a stale row from clock skew or replication lag can't paint as "live"), suppresses to null when `paymentClaimPrompt` targets the same invoice id (the client-claims-they-paid signal is stronger and more action-specific), null-followUpIntents fallback when public_token is missing or malformed, and exports `RECENT_VIEW_WINDOW_MINUTES` for tests + future configurability. The 48h `clientViewedFollowupPrompt` gains a reciprocal suppression: when `recentViewPrompt` fires on the same invoice id, the stale follow-up suppresses so the freelancer sees one banner per invoice with the strongest available framing. New view block in `views/dashboard.ejs` between `paymentClaimPrompt` and `clientViewedFollowupPrompt` (fuchsia tone for "live now" visual distinction from the emerald paid-claim / 48h-follow-up palette, a pulsing live-dot indicator, "X minutes ago" / "just now" microcopy, "🔥 Your client is looking at your invoice right now" hero, a primary "Open & send a check-in" CTA, a secondary "They paid? Mark as paid" form posting to `/invoices/:id/status` with CSRF, and an inline three-button share-intent row — WhatsApp / SMS / Copy — each firing `POST /invoices/:id/share-intent` with `keepalive:true` + the matching intent kind + CSRF, so a single tap fires a follow-up nudge through the channel the freelancer prefers without leaving the dashboard). The share-intent buttons use the `followUpIntents` body shape ("Hi <Client>, just checking in on invoice X — let me know if you have any questions"), not the first-send body shape, so the wording matches the situation (the client has already seen the invoice). Copy button writes the URL to `navigator.clipboard` and shows a transient "Copied!" label. Test coverage: 49 assertions in `tests/recent-view-prompt.test.js` across five layers — (1) SQL contract: status filter + is_seed=false + last_viewed_at IS NOT NULL + within-window predicate + DESC LIMIT 1 + params + default 60 + fractional-flooring + invalid-input fallback + null-userId short-circuit + projection includes the public_token/view_count/last_viewed_at columns; (2) loadMostRecentlyViewedUnpaid soft-fail paths; (3) buildRecentViewPrompt shape contract (null user / null invoice / null id / null last_viewed_at / unparseable date / stale >60min defence-in-depth / minutesAgo computation / viewCount coercion / stringy total / empty client_name / paymentClaimPrompt suppression same vs different invoice / follow-up share-intents derivation / malformed public_token graceful null); (4) `clientViewedFollowupPrompt` reciprocal suppression (same invoice → null, different invoice → unchanged); (5) view layer (banner omit/render contract, named + empty-client copy paths, invoice number + `$X.XX` total + minutesAgo + "just now" microcopy + view-count line conditional render, primary CTA href, mark-paid form POSTs status=paid + CSRF, share-intent buttons omitted-when-null + rendered-with-correct-hrefs + onclick wires to POST /share-intent with intent kind + CSRF + clipboard write, XSS escape on hostile client_name AND on hostile share URL, positional contract above clientViewedFollowupPrompt and below paymentClaimPrompt, data attributes for hooks, print:hidden, live-dot indicator). Full suite: 101 test files, 0 failures.
Advances: Milestone 4 (first invoice sent → first payment received). The existing M4 surfaces fire on stale signals: `clientViewedFollowupPrompt` only triggers at 48h+ since first view (days-old framing); `sentNotViewedPrompt` covers the silent-failure cohort at 72h+; `overduePrompt` fires on contractual due-date. None of them cover the LIVE cohort — a client actively looking at the invoice right now, which is the single highest payment-intent moment we can detect. A freelancer who lands on the dashboard while their client's `/i/<token>` tab is still open (or was open in the last hour) sees a one-tap path to send a polite check-in through the channel of their choice, while the invoice is still on the client's screen and in their working memory. Each recovered nudge fires through the existing `markInvoiceSentFromShareIntent` + share-intent stamping path, and each click-through compounds into the dashboard's `payment_claim_prompt` → mark-paid flow when the client follows through. Works across all plans — free / trial / Pro / Agency — the share-intent surface is plan-agnostic so the free cohort (no Stripe payment link, no /email-client button) gets the same live-conversion lever Pro users get. Activation-funnel impact: every recovered live-view → paid transition flips `sent_one` → `got_paid`, the final stage in the `lib/activation-funnel` stage definition, which is the Primary Objective's "Done means" terminal observable.
Master action: none — pure-code update (1 idempotent db helper + 1 route helper + 1 builder + 1 reciprocal suppression line + 1 view block + 1 test file + 1 line in package.json). No schema migration (the `last_viewed_at` column was already added by the public-invoice-view-tracking ship and stamped by `recordPublicInvoiceView`). ## 2026-05-25
Shipped: one-tap WhatsApp / SMS / Copy-link share-intent buttons inline in the dashboard's fresh-draft prompt — backed by an eager `public_token` mint at create time on POST `/invoices/quick` AND POST `/invoices/new`. Collapses the "I just made a draft, now send it" activation step from three clicks (dashboard prompt → /invoices/:id → tap a channel) to one tap straight from the dashboard, which matters most on mobile cohorts that re-enter via the welcome email / no-invoice-nudge / second-no-invoice-nudge / pending-quick-invoice-nudge magic-login URLs and land on the dashboard before opening the draft. The mint pair runs immediately after `db.createInvoice` returns, wrapped in try/catch with a soft-fail warn — a transient pg failure does NOT block the redirect (the legacy lazy-mint on GET `/invoices/:id` remains the fallback). `buildFreshDraftPrompt` is extended to derive `shareIntents` via the existing `lib/share-link.buildShareSurfaceForInvoice` helper when the row carries a valid `public_token` (the regex on `lib/share-link` rejects malformed tokens, so a token leaked from a non-standard source can't produce a usable URL); the returned `shareIntents` exposes `whatsapp` (`https://wa.me/?text=<encoded body>`), `sms` (`sms:?&body=<encoded body>`), and `url` (the absolute `/i/<token>` page URL) — the body string includes the greeting, invoice number, $amount, and the public URL, kept short enough to fit the SMS 160-char preview window. The dashboard view adds a third action row under the existing "Open & send invoice →" + "Mark as sent" buttons, gated on `freshDraftPrompt.shareIntents && freshDraftPrompt.shareIntents.url` (silently omitted when null, so legacy drafts without a token degrade cleanly to the deep-link CTA). Each button fires `POST /invoices/<id>/share-intent` with `keepalive:true` + `X-CSRF-Token` + the matching intent kind in the JSON body — the existing share-intent endpoint atomically flips `status='draft'` → `'sent'` via `markInvoiceSentFromShareIntent` and triggers the first-sent celebration email (idempotent at the SQL layer, safe to call from any surface). The Copy button additionally writes the URL to `navigator.clipboard` and shows a transient "Copied!" label. Test coverage: 4 new assertions in `tests/fresh-draft-prompt.test.js` for the builder (null token → null intents, valid token → whatsapp+sms+url shape, malformed token rejected) + 5 new view assertions (intent row omitted/rendered, anchor hrefs verbatim, all three buttons wired to POST /share-intent with their kind and CSRF, hostile URL is HTML-attribute-escaped). 2 new assertions in `tests/invoice-quick.test.js` for the eager mint (called exactly once with the new invoice id, mint failure does NOT block the redirect). One housekeeping fix: `tests/invoice-crud.test.js` db stub gained `getOrCreatePublicToken` + `getRecentClientsForUser` no-op methods to silence the now-warning paths on POST `/invoices/new`. Full suite: 100 test files, 0 failures.
Advances: Milestone 3 (first invoice created → first invoice sent) — the activation funnel's biggest single drop-off, named explicitly in PLAN.md. Today's fresh-draft prompt fires inside the same 0–24h window the user is most likely to actually send (peak attention, "what was I just doing?"), but until now its only call-to-action was a deep-link to `/invoices/:id` — a full page navigation, slow on flaky mobile data, that drops a non-trivial share of returning users at the in-between page load. With this ship, a freelancer who arrives on the dashboard via the welcome / no-invoice-nudge / magic-login flow sees the prompt AND has a tap-WhatsApp / tap-SMS / tap-Copy surface immediately available, all wired to the existing atomic `draft → sent` flip so the act of sharing the link IS the act of marking it sent. Compounds with every Milestone 4 surface downstream (client-viewed prompt + email, sent-not-viewed prompt + nudge, overdue prompt + digest, payment-claim prompt + 48h follow-up, paid-receipt-to-client email, repeat-client prompt) because every recovered first-send flips activation funnel `created_real → sent_one` and unlocks the full M4 conversion cascade. Plan-agnostic — free, trial, Pro, and Agency cohorts all benefit; the share-intent surface is the only end-to-end send path the free tier has (no Stripe payment link, no /email-client button), so this surface IS the activation lever for the free cohort.
Master action: none.

## 2026-05-24
Shipped: "Send test to my inbox" button on `/invoices/:id/preview-email` so a freelancer with a draft can verify how the email actually renders in their real mail client (Gmail / Apple Mail / Outlook) before tapping "send to client". New `lib/email.sendInvoiceTestEmail(invoice, owner)` — recipient hardcoded to `owner.email` (never reads `invoice.client_email`, no spam vector); subject prefixed with `[Test] ` so inbox search can distinguish the preview from real client copies; identical html/text body, identical reply-to, no `List-Unsubscribe` footer (transactional self-directed). New `POST /invoices/:id/email-self` (auth + ownership-gated via `db.getInvoiceById`'s `user_id` filter): no plan gate (free, trial, Pro, Agency — activation is the goal and we don't gate the de-anxiety surface), no `client_email` requirement (send goes to the owner so a freelancer mid-edit can still preview), never flips invoice status, never fires the first-sent celebration, never auto-creates a payment link. Response carries `{ ok, sent_to, message_id }` + `Cache-Control: no-store`. Error envelope: 401 unauthorized when `getUserById` returns null, 400 `no_owner_email` when the user row has null email, 404 `not_found` on cross-tenant or unknown invoice id, 503 `not_configured` when `RESEND_API_KEY` is unset, 502 on a generic Resend failure. View update at `views/invoice-preview-email.ejs`: an "actions" row inside the chrome banner renders the button + a status span (hidden by default). An inline `<script data-testid="preview-email-self-script">` wires button-click → fetch POST `/invoices/<id>/email-self` with the `X-CSRF-Token` header, disables the button before the fetch (defence against a panicked double-tap), and maps the failure reasons to human-readable copy (`not_configured` → "Test send is not available yet…", `no_owner_email` → "No email address on file…"). Both the button and the script are gated on `user && user.email`. Test coverage: 17 new assertions in `tests/invoice-self-test-email.test.js` across 4 layers — (1) lib: send targets owner.email and NEVER `invoice.client_email`, `[Test]` subject prefix, byte-for-byte html/text parity with `sendInvoiceEmail` (locks in the preview-fidelity contract); (2) lib: missing owner email and missing invoice both soft-fail with no Resend send attempted; (3) route: happy path 200 + sent_to + message_id + no status flip (drift-guard: `markInvoiceSentFromShareIntent` is stubbed to fail loudly if a future refactor copies email-client's flip onto this path) + Cache-Control no-store, free plan parity, missing `client_email` still 200, cross-tenant 404, unknown id 404, missing user 401, missing owner.email 400, Resend not_configured 503, generic Resend error 502; (4) view: button renders with owner email label + invoice id + csrf attrs + status span hidden, inline script wires to `/email-self` with X-CSRF-Token + button disables before fetch + sent_to drives the success label, no button or script when owner.email is missing. Full suite: 100 files, 0 failures.
Advances: Milestone 3 (first invoice created → first invoice sent) — the activation funnel's biggest single drop-off, named explicitly in PLAN.md. Today's surfaces for the "stuck in draft" cohort attack the bounce path (24h stale-draft dashboard prompt + 24h stale-draft email + 7d second-stale-draft email + on-page draft-send-banner with the `target=_blank` preview-email link) but don't attack the underlying pre-send anxiety — "I don't know what my client will actually see in Gmail/Apple Mail/Outlook". The static iframe preview at `/invoices/:id/preview-email` is helpful but it's an isolated render on a white page that doesn't show how the email behaves in a real inbox: dark-mode rendering, Gmail's preview-pane truncation, the Apple Mail link tap target, the Outlook reply-quote framing, the actual subject row in the inbox list, the spam-folder placement signal. One tap on "Send test to my inbox" resolves every one of those unknowns in 30 seconds: the freelancer opens their own real mail client, sees exactly what their client will see, and either tweaks the invoice (notes, business name, payment instructions) or — far more often — hits "Email to client" with the confidence they were missing. Compounds with every Milestone 3 surface already shipped (stale-draft prompts, fresh-draft prompt, draft-send banner, share-intent buttons, email-client send) because each recovered draft → sent flips activation funnel `created_real` → `sent_one`, and every flip downstream unlocks the full Milestone 4 cascade (client-viewed prompt, sent-not-viewed nudge, overdue digest, payment-claim widget, paid-receipt email, repeat-client prompt). Works across all plans — free, trial, Pro, Agency — because anxiety doesn't pay attention to plan tier, and activation is the cohort that compounds against every downstream conversion lever.
Master action: none.

## 2026-05-24
Shipped: self-serve lifecycle-email unsubscribe end-to-end (CAN-SPAM compliance + RFC 8058 one-click `List-Unsubscribe` support). New `users.unsubscribe_token VARCHAR(32) UNIQUE` + `users.lifecycle_emails_opted_out_at TIMESTAMP` columns (idempotent ALTER TABLE migration); new `lib/unsubscribe.js` (token validity + URL builder + RFC 2369/8058 header pair + html/text footer injector + lazy-mint-from-row helper); new `db.getOrCreateUnsubscribeToken` (mirrors the `getOrCreateReferralCode` / `getOrCreatePublicToken` pattern: SELECT-then-UPDATE with UNIQUE-violation retry up to 3×, stable forever — never rotated, because the value of an unsubscribe link rises with age and a rotating token would orphan every previously-sent email); new `db.findUserByUnsubscribeToken`, `db.markLifecycleOptOut`, `db.markLifecycleResubscribe` (idempotent by construction); new `routes/unsubscribe.js` with `GET /unsubscribe/:token` (confirm-then-POST page so bot link-scanners don't silently opt people out), `POST /unsubscribe/:token` (RFC 8058 one-click target — stamps the opt-out), and `POST /unsubscribe/:token/resubscribe` (clears the stamp); new `views/unsubscribe.ejs` + `views/unsubscribed.ejs` (success page exposes the resubscribe button so a misclick is one-tap-recoverable). `lib/email.sendEmail` now accepts `{ unsubscribeUrl, headers }` — when `unsubscribeUrl` is supplied it injects the `List-Unsubscribe: <url>` + `List-Unsubscribe-Post: List-Unsubscribe=One-Click` header pair AND appends a one-line opt-out footer to both html (slotted before `</body>`) and text bodies. `middleware/csrf.js` grows a prefix-scoped bypass for `/unsubscribe/` (mail clients posting one-click POSTs never carry session cookies — authority is the 16-hex opaque token in the URL, ~2^64 enumeration space); the bypass is tightly prefix-scoped so sibling POSTs under the same session stay CSRF-protected (regression-asserted). Every lifecycle cron's cohort SQL (`getUsersForNoInvoiceNudge`, `getUsersForSecondNoInvoiceNudge`, `getUsersForPendingQuickInvoiceNudge`, `getUsersWithStaleDraftForEmail`, `getUsersForSecondStaleDraftEmail`, `getTrialUsersNeedingNudge`, `getUsersWithOverdueInvoicesForDigest`, `getInvoicesForClientViewedFollowup`, `getInvoicesForSentNotViewedNudge`, `getInvoicesForPaymentClaimFollowup`) gains `AND u.lifecycle_emails_opted_out_at IS NULL` and adds `u.unsubscribe_token` to the SELECT so the orchestrator threads it through with zero extra round-trips. `markWelcomeEmailSent` RETURNING grows `unsubscribe_token` and `lib/welcome.triggerWelcomeEmail` now best-effort-resolves the URL via `resolveUnsubscribeUrlForRow` and passes it into `sendWelcomeEmail`. All 10 cron orchestrators (no-invoice / second-no-invoice / pending-quick-invoice / stale-draft / second-stale-draft / trial / overdue-digest / client-viewed-followup / sent-not-viewed / pending-payment-claim) call `resolveUnsubscribeUrlForRow` per row (zero round-trips when the SELECT already surfaced the token, lazy-mint via `getOrCreateUnsubscribeToken` when the row token is NULL, soft-fails to a footer-less send when the mint hiccups — the nudge is NEVER sacrificed to a mint failure). Transactional emails (invoice-to-client, paid-receipt-to-client, magic-login, password-reset, paid-notification, client-viewed real-time, payment-claimed real-time, referral-celebration, first-sent-celebration) are deliberately NOT footer-injected or opt-out-gated — they fire on specific user-initiated events and remain legally transactional. Test coverage: 21 new assertions in `tests/unsubscribe.test.js` across 5 layers — (1) lib pure helpers (token validity edges including XSS payloads, URL construction with + without APP_URL, RFC 8058 header shape, footer injection that lands inside or appended to body-less HTML, lazy-mint via row); (2) `sendEmail` extension (headers + footer present when `unsubscribeUrl` is passed, absent when it isn't — locking in that transactional sends stay clean); (3) route surface (GET renders email + form, already-opted-out banner shows when stamp exists, bad token 404s without a DB hit, unknown valid token 404s, POST with no session + no CSRF token succeeds proving the one-click path works, POST /resubscribe clears the stamp); (4) CSRF prefix-bypass regression — sibling POSTs under the same app are STILL rejected, so the bypass cannot leak globally; (5) cron-gate regression — the production SQL for `getUsersForNoInvoiceNudge` and `getInvoicesForClientViewedFollowup` both contain `lifecycle_emails_opted_out_at IS NULL` AND `unsubscribe_token` in the SELECT (drift-guard: any future maintenance edit that drops either fails this test loudly), plus the no-invoice orchestrator threads the per-row URL into `sendEmail` (lazy mint exercised for the token-NULL branch). Full suite: 83 files, 0 failures.
Advances: Primary Objective (signup → first-sent-invoice activation rate). Lifecycle email deliverability is the multiplier on every cron-driven re-engagement and activation surface shipped over the last 30 sessions (welcome, 2× no-invoice, pending-quick-invoice, 2× stale-draft, trial, overdue-digest, client-viewed-followup, sent-not-viewed, pending-payment-claim — 11 emails). Without an `List-Unsubscribe` header pair, Gmail / Apple Mail / Outlook treat high-volume re-engagement sends as spam-signal: bulk-folder placement, ~20-40% delivery degradation across the cohort, and a downward sender-reputation spiral that infects even the transactional paid-receipt / magic-login emails. With this ship, every lifecycle email carries the RFC 8058 one-click header so mail clients route the "Unsubscribe" gesture cleanly, the recipient's choice is honoured by the very next cron tick (gated at the SQL layer, not the JS-orchestrator layer — so a future refactor can't accidentally bypass it), and the sender domain reputation stays clean for the long-haul cohort. Net effect on activation: every email in the funnel reaches more inboxes, the proportion that lands in spam drops materially, and angry-reply / spam-complaint rates fall (a single user who has no opt-out path will mark the next email as spam — which damages delivery for the whole cohort, not just their own row). Also unlocks launch — CAN-SPAM §5(a)(5) requires every commercial email carry a functioning opt-out mechanism, and Stripe ToS / app-store reviewer checklists both flag missing unsubscribe as a launch blocker.
Master action: none code-wise — schema migration is idempotent (`ALTER TABLE … ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS`) and lands on the next operator `heroku pg:psql < db/schema.sql` per the existing MASTER_ACTIONS Deploy block.
