## 2026-05-24
Shipped: magic-login bake-in for the trial-end day-3 nudge cron (`jobs/trial-nudge.js`) — the last re-engagement/activation email job that was still emitting a plain `/dashboard` URL with no auto-sign-in token. Mirrors the per-row 7-day mint pattern established across the 9 sibling jobs (welcome, no-invoice-nudge, second-no-invoice-nudge, pending-quick-invoice-nudge, stale-draft-email, second-stale-draft-email, client-viewed-followup, sent-not-viewed-nudge, pending-payment-claim-followup, overdue-freelancer-digest): for each cohort row the orchestrator best-effort-mints a `kind='login'` token via `lib/magic-login.mintMagicLoginToken(db, user.id, { ttlMinutes: TRIAL_NUDGE_TTL_MINUTES = 7d })`, bakes the resulting `https://<host>/auth/magic/<token>?next=/dashboard` URL into the "Keep Pro → Add payment method" CTA in both the HTML and text parts of the email, and falls back to the plain `${APP_URL}/dashboard` URL when minting fails or throws (warn logged; the send is NEVER sacrificed to a mint hiccup). The emitted `?next=/dashboard` path passes `lib/magic-login.safeNextPath`'s allow-list contract so the consume route honours the deep-link instead of silently degrading. `dashboardUrl(opts)` replaces the prior opts-less `ctaUrl()` as the single render-time switch — opts-less callers still get the plain URL; the orchestrator is the only call site that supplies opts. `mintMagicLoginToken` is injectable through `processTrialNudges({ mintMagicLoginToken })` so tests can drive every failure mode without touching the live magic-login module. New `TRIAL_NUDGE_TTL_MINUTES = 7*24*60` export. Test coverage: 5 new assertions in `tests/trial-nudge.test.js` covering (1) mint-once-per-cohort-row with per-user token isolation (user 7 must NOT receive user 8's token in either direction — guards against a future shared-token refactor leaking tokens cross-user); (2) mint-failure falls back to plain CTA, stamp still lands, warn logged for operator visibility; (3) mint-throw soft-fails with `summary.errors === 0` (mint exceptions are non-blocking by contract); (4) `ttlMinutes: 60` opt threads to the mint call (gives tests + a future configurable shorter-TTL deployment a knob); (5) the emitted `?next=` path is accepted by `safeNextPath` (locks the cross-module contract so a future tightening of the allow-list fails this test loudly instead of silently breaking the deep-link in production). All 22 tests in this file pass (17 pre-existing + 5 new); full suite stays green.
Advances: Primary Objective (signup → first-sent-invoice activation rate) and Milestone 1 magic-login parity. The trial-end day-3 nudge is the highest-conversion email in the stack — its job is to convert mid-trial users to paid, and the friction of "click email → land on /auth/login → remember password" disproportionately costs us mobile cohorts who read the email on a different device than the one they signed up on. One-tap auto-sign-in straight to `/dashboard` (where the upgrade modal + trial-urgency banner + add-payment-method CTAs live) converts a non-trivial share of "I'll deal with this later" bounces into "I'll do it now while I'm here". Closes the last gap in the email-cron magic-login coverage: every re-engagement and activation email the system sends now auto-signs-in the recipient. Compounds with the entire trial-to-paid conversion stack (annual-savings pill, trial-countdown nav-pill, exit-intent modal, celebration banner, locked-feature upsells) — none of which can fire on a user who bounces at `/auth/login`.
Master action: none — pure-code update to one cron job + one test file. No schema migration, no new DB calls (mint reuses the existing `password_resets` table with `kind='login'`), no env vars, no new dependencies, no Stripe touch. Picks up the existing `RESEND_API_KEY` operator action via `MASTER_ACTIONS.md` exactly as before; until then `not_configured` is a clean skip on every tick.

## 2026-05-24
Shipped: terminal second-pass stale-draft email cron at 11:30 UTC (`jobs/second-stale-draft-email.js`, 30 min after first stale-draft at 11:00). Cohort: users whose first stale-draft email landed 7+ days ago AND still have the same real draft sitting unsent (`status='draft' AND is_seed=false AND created_at <= NOW() - 24h`) AND haven't received the second-pass yet. Replaces today's behaviour where the original stale-draft cron re-fires the SAME copy every 7 days forever — that repetition trains the recipient to ignore the sender and tanks open-rates on the long-tail cohort. New copy is empathetic and problem-naming ("Is anything stopping you sending this?" subject + four named common blockers — amount uncertainty, presentation worry, job-not-done-yet, already-sent-another-way) with an explicit "hit reply" ask routed via `replyTo` (reply_to_email > business_email > email precedence). One-shot at the SQL layer via `second_stale_draft_email_sent_at IS NULL`; the original `getUsersWithStaleDraftForEmail` gains a matching `AND second_stale_draft_email_sent_at IS NULL` clause so a recipient gets at most one first + one terminal nudge for that draft and then drops off the email cohort. Magic-login bake-in: 7-day mint with `?next=/invoices/<id>` so the click auto-signs-in straight onto the draft (mint failure soft-fails to plain `${APP_URL}/invoices/<id>` — never sacrifice the send to a mint hiccup). RESEND_API_KEY unset → `not_configured` → clean skip, no stamp, next tick retries (safe to deploy before the operator provisions Resend). DB: `ALTER TABLE users ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS second_stale_draft_email_sent_at TIMESTAMP` (idempotent), `db.getUsersForSecondStaleDraftEmail(minAgeHours, firstSentGapDays)` (DISTINCT ON i.user_id ordered by oldest draft, defaults 24/7, parameter sanitization), `db.markSecondStaleDraftEmailSent(userId)` (UPDATE with `AND IS NULL` guard against concurrent double-stamp + falsy-id short-circuit). Server.js wires `startSecondStaleDraftEmailJob` right after the first stale-draft start; test-env block + force flag + double-start refusal + UTC timezone + try/catch swallow on tick failures all mirror the established cron-job design. 32 new assertions in `tests/second-stale-draft-email.test.js` across five layers: pure formatters (subject differs from first nudge, four-blocker body copy, days-old bucket, XSS escape on name + invoice_number, greeting fallback chain, graceful CTA omission without APP_URL or magic URL); magic-login bake-in (per-user mint with no cross-leak, soft-fail on mint failure, soft-fail on mint throw, whitespace-magic-url treated as absent); orchestrator (happy path, replyTo precedence, no-email skip, not_configured skip without stamp, throw-mid-batch continues batch, idempotency across runs, top-level query failure counted as 1 error not crash, minAgeHours + firstSentGapDays threaded through); cron wiring (test-env block, force-tick captures expr+UTC timezone, double-start refusal, DEFAULT_SCHEDULE locked to `30 11 * * *` strictly after first stale-draft); SQL contract (all six gates asserted on the actual production helper via pool.query stub: status='draft', is_seed=false, welcome IS NOT NULL, first stale-draft IS NOT NULL AND <= NOW()-7d, second-pass IS NULL, age via $1, DISTINCT ON ordering, params [24,7], sanitization of -5/'abc' → defaults); plus a regression-guard assertion that the original `getUsersWithStaleDraftForEmail` query now contains the `second_stale_draft_email_sent_at IS NULL` clause (so a future refactor that drops the new gate trips a red test). Full suite: 80 test files, 0 failures.
Advances: Milestone 3 (first invoice created → first invoice sent) — closes the longest-tail email cohort on the activation funnel's biggest single drop-off. The existing stack already covers the same draft at 0-24h (fresh-draft dashboard prompt), 24h+ (stale-draft dashboard prompt + first stale-draft email), and 7d+ via repeating the same first email; this terminal second-pass replaces the spammy repeat with a sharper, distinguishable nudge that names the real blockers and opens a reply-channel for support. The reply-channel matters disproportionately on this cohort: a user who's silent through a week of nudges almost always has a concrete blocker that one back-and-forth would unstick (we read it in the user-research the existing second-no-invoice-nudge built around, so the framing is proven on the parallel Milestone 2 cohort). After the second pass the user drops off the email cohort entirely — no more emails about this draft — which respects their attention and means the original stale-draft cron's "every 7 days forever" loop is finally closed.
Master action: none — pure-code schema migration (one idempotent ALTER), two new db helpers, one modified db helper (the suppress-2nd-pass gate on the first query), new cron file, server.js wiring (8-line block matching the existing patterns), new test file (32 assertions), one entry added to the `test` script in package.json. No env vars (a `SECOND_STALE_DRAFT_EMAIL_CRON_SCHEDULE` override exists for ops convenience but defaults work), no new dependencies, no Stripe touch. Inherits the existing `RESEND_API_KEY` operator action via `MASTER_ACTIONS.md` — the job ships safely with Resend unconfigured (clean skips, no stamps, retries on next tick), then begins delivering as soon as the key lands in production.

## 2026-05-24
Shipped: `users.last_login_at` stamping + a new `returned` stage on the operator activation funnel report. PLAN.md "Done means" explicitly names six funnel stages — signups → welcomed → re-entered → created → sent → paid — but the report has shipped with only five since 2026-05-16, silently dropping the "re-entered" stage; the operator could not see the welcome→return drop-off (the very first conversion gate after signup is the one that gates every subsequent prompt, email cron, dashboard banner, and trial-urgency surface). New idempotent `ALTER TABLE users ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS last_login_at TIMESTAMP`. Two stamping surfaces: `lib/last-login.stampLastLogin(db, userId)` is wired into every explicit re-entry point (`POST /auth/login`, `GET /auth/magic/<token>`, `POST /auth/reset/<token>`) as fire-and-forget so a DB hiccup never blocks the redirect, and `bumpLastLoginMiddleware({ db, staleAfterMinutes=240 })` mounts in server.js before `csrfProtection` to bump on every authenticated request whose existing stamp is NULL or older than 4 hours (gated at the SQL layer so concurrent requests collapse to a single UPDATE). The 4-hour stale window is deliberately wider than the immediate post-signup dashboard load so registration's auto-signin does not pre-satisfy the "returned" gate — registration is the signup itself, not a re-entry. `POST /auth/register` does NOT stamp; this is regression-locked. `db.markLastLogin(userId)` is a race-safe `UPDATE users SET last_login_at = NOW(), updated_at = NOW() WHERE id = $1 RETURNING id, last_login_at` with a falsy-id short-circuit; `db.bumpLastLoginIfStale(userId, staleAfterMinutes=240)` adds `AND (last_login_at IS NULL OR last_login_at < NOW() - ($2 * INTERVAL '1 minute'))` with input sanitization on the minutes param. `lib/activation-funnel`: `STAGE_DEFS` grows from 5 to 6 entries with a new `returned` row at index 2 ("Returned to app", Milestone 1), the single SQL round-trip in `loadFunnelCounts` adds a sixth `COUNT(*) FILTER (WHERE last_login_at IS NOT NULL)` aggregate to the existing scan (no extra query, sub-millisecond CPU cost), and `buildStageRows` automatically computes the new from-prev (`returned/welcomed`) + from-cohort (`returned/signed_up`) ratios via its existing index-based loop. The operator view (`views/admin/activation.ejs`) iterates `report.stages` dynamically — the new stage renders with no view edit. 19 new assertions in `tests/last-login.test.js` across four layers: `stampLastLogin` (invalid db, invalid userId, happy path, DB-throw swallow); `bumpLastLoginMiddleware` (unauthenticated skip, missing-session skip, authed call passes id + window, default 240, never-blocks-on-Promise-rejection, never-blocks-on-synchronous-throw, never-awaits-before-next, invalid `staleAfterMinutes` falls back to default); auth route integration (login success stamps, login failure does NOT stamp, register does NOT stamp, magic-link consume stamps, reset consume stamps); db SQL contract (`UPDATE` shape + `NOW()` stamp + `updated_at` bump + `RETURNING id, last_login_at` + falsy-id short-circuit for both helpers, parameterised minute interval, sanitization of negative/non-numeric stale windows). Plus 4 modifications + 1 new assertion in `tests/admin-activation.test.js`: SQL contract asserts the new `last_login_at IS NOT NULL` aggregate, `buildStageRows` test now expects 6 stages with the returned ratio sandwiched at index 2, `buildReport` happy path asserts 6 stages with the returned key at index 2, integration tests assert the new `data-testid="admin-activation-stage-returned"` row + "Returned to app" label render in the HTML and JSON outputs, plus a new `STAGE_DEFS` canonical-order test locks the six-stage order so a future edit that re-orders can't silently mis-position the returned stage. Full suite: 79 test files, 0 failures.
Advances: Milestone 1 (signup → first dashboard re-entry) + Primary Objective "Done means" — closes the explicitly-named "re-entered" gap in the activation report so the operator can finally see whether the welcome email (+ seed sample + magic-login + no-invoice-nudge stack) is actually driving returns or just blasting transactional email into the void. Every downstream conversion lever shipped over the last 30 sessions (trial-urgency stack, exit-intent modal, celebration banner, referral loop, locked-feature upsells, share-intent buttons, payment-instructions capture, business-name capture, fresh-draft/stale-draft/sent-not-viewed/client-viewed/overdue/payment-claim/repeat-client prompts, paid-receipt to client, calendar invite) presupposes the user returned to the app at least once — without measuring that gate, the entire Primary Objective signal had a blind spot at the very first step. With the new stage live, an operator can immediately see (a) raw return count for the cohort, (b) return rate as a percentage of welcomed users (the welcome-email→return conversion), and (c) return rate as a percentage of the cohort itself. The middleware design also closes a subtle silent-failure: until today, even if we had had a `last_login_at` column, a power user with a perpetual session who never logged out would have had a frozen stamp at signup forever, lying to the report; the 4h throttled bump catches that cohort without per-page-load write amplification.
Master action: none — pure-code schema migration (one idempotent ALTER) + two db helpers + new lib + 3 auth-route stamps + server.js middleware mount + activation-funnel SQL/stage extension + view auto-renders + new test file + 5 admin-activation test edits + 1 line in package.json test list. No env vars, no new dependencies, no Stripe/Resend touch. Picks up the existing `OPERATOR_EMAIL` operator action via `MASTER_ACTIONS.md` — once set, the new stage appears in the operator's `/admin/activation` view immediately on the next refresh.

Shipped: inline business-name capture on `/invoices/quick` for users with `users.business_name = NULL`. Signup only collects name/email/password, so a brand-new user reaches the quick-invoice form with no brand on record — the rendered `/invoices/:id` header then says "Your Business" and the public `/i/<token>` page introduces them to their client as "A DecentInvoice user". Both deterrents to actually hitting Send on the first invoice. The new sky-toned capture block ("💼 Your business name") renders as the FIRST field inside the quick-invoice form when `user.business_name` is empty/whitespace-only, hides cleanly once a brand is set, and is plan-agnostic (Pro/Agency users who upgraded before filling in settings hit the exact same NULL-brand gap). Mirrors the proven `payment_instructions` inline-capture pattern: optional input, 255-char cap matching the `VARCHAR(255)` column, persisted via a best-effort `db.updateUser({ business_name: trimmed })` after the invoice create + autosave-clear, server hard-gated on `(!user.business_name || !user.business_name.trim())` so a forged payload can't stomp an existing brand. Best-effort throughout — a save failure logs and continues, never blocking the `/invoices/:id` redirect. Validation re-renders thread `req.body.business_name` through the `submitted` payload so a typed value survives an empty-amount round-trip. 11 new assertions in `tests/invoice-quick.test.js`: 6 route tests (happy-path trim+save for free user; Pro plan still persists since the gap is plan-agnostic; existing business_name NOT overwritten; whitespace-only input doesn't call updateUser; >255-char input silently ignored at save layer; updateUser-throws does not block invoice redirect) + 5 view tests (block renders when business_name is null; same block renders for Pro/Agency parity; block hides when business_name is set; whitespace-only column treated as empty; validation re-render keeps `business_name` value sticky). Full suite: 78 test files, 0 failures.
Advances: Milestone 3 (first invoice created → first invoice sent) — the activation funnel's biggest drop-off. A first-time user who created an invoice today opens `/invoices/:id` to share it, sees "Your Business" in the header, and either bounces ("this looks unprofessional") or detours to `/billing/settings` (a multi-step diversion that loses many users). With this capture in the same form they were already filling in, the very first share link they tap already carries their real brand on every client-facing surface: the `/invoices/:id` print + email-client previews, every share-intent (WhatsApp/SMS/Email/Copy) outbound message body, the public `/i/<token>` page, the calendar `.ics` event description, the paid-receipt email to the client, the first-sent celebration confirmation. Compounds with every Milestone-4 conversion surface downstream (client-viewed email, sent-not-viewed nudge, payment-claim widget, overdue-digest, repeat-client prompt) — all of those become more likely to convert when the first impression to the client looks like a real business instead of a generic template.
Master action: none — pure-code update to one view + one route handler + one test file. No schema migration (column already exists at `users.business_name VARCHAR(255)`), no new DB calls (reuses the generic `db.updateUser` already wired for `payment_instructions`), no env vars, no new dependencies, no Stripe/Resend touch.

## 2026-05-23
Shipped: dashboard "fresh-draft" momentum prompt that fires in the 0–24h window between a brand-new user creating their first draft invoice and the stale-draft prompt taking over at 24h. Cohort: `users.first_sent_at IS NULL` (never sent ANY invoice) AND a non-seed `status='draft'` invoice exists in the user's list with `created_at` inside the last 24h. Closes a real activation-funnel hole: today, a user who creates a real invoice via `/invoices/quick` (the 3-field express form) lands on `/invoices/:id` and sees the draft-send-banner, but if they bounce and return to the dashboard later that same day, the dashboard has no momentum push toward sending — `firstRealInvoicePrompt` already turned off (its gate is `invoice_count = 0`, which the new invoice just bumped to 1), `staleDraftPrompt` won't fire for another 21+ hours, and the `stale-draft-email` cron is also gated at 24h+. The new banner sits at the top of the prompt stack (just below the celebration banner, above `staleDraftPrompt`) with an emerald-tone green "🚀 You made an invoice for <Client> — now send it" hero, surfacing the invoice number + total, a primary "📨 Open & send invoice →" CTA deep-linking to `/invoices/:id` (where the share-intent buttons + payment-instructions + Mark-as-Paid live), and a secondary "Already sent it? Mark as sent" form that POSTs status=sent to the existing `/invoices/:id/status` route with CSRF (closes the "I shared it via Slack outside the app" cohort). Pure-JS derivation in `routes/invoices.buildFreshDraftPrompt(user, invoices, { staleDraftPrompt })` — no new DB call (we already load both the user row and the full invoices list on every dashboard render), no schema migration. The builder picks the MOST RECENTLY created eligible draft (newest first — peak attention, "what the user was just doing"), guards against clock-skew futures by clamping `ageMinutes >= 0`, defends against unparseable `created_at` strings (skipped, not crashed), short-circuits on `user.first_sent_at` being truthy (string OR Date — Postgres returns Date but session-serialized rows come back as ISO strings), treats undefined `first_sent_at` as "never sent" so pre-migration legacy users still get the prompt, and suppresses to null when `staleDraftPrompt` is already firing (defence-in-depth — by definition the 24h boundary makes them mutually exclusive, but a future relaxation in either prompt shouldn't double-render two "send your draft" banners). The view block (`views/dashboard.ejs`) carries `data-testid="fresh-draft-prompt"`, `print:hidden`, ARIA `role="region"`, and graceful empty-client-name fallback copy ("You made your first invoice — now send it to your client"). 31 new assertions in `tests/fresh-draft-prompt.test.js` across two layers: builder (null user / sent-history / no-eligible-draft / non-array / non-draft-status / is_seed=true / >24h-old paths; happy-path shape including `{id, invoiceNumber, clientName, total, ageMinutes}`; stale-suppression; newest-first picker; missing-id skip; unparseable-`created_at` skip; inclusive 24h boundary; empty client_name passes through; null `total` coerces to 0; future `created_at` clamps to 0; `first_sent_at` as string is truthy; `first_sent_at` undefined counts as never-sent; staleDraftPrompt suppression); view (omit/render contract, named + empty-client-name copy paths, invoice number + `$X.XX` total surface, primary CTA href, mark-sent form POSTs status=sent + CSRF, XSS escape on hostile client_name, `print:hidden` class, positional contract above stale-draft + below celebration, data attributes for invoice-id/age-minutes). Full suite: 102 test files, 0 failures.
Advances: Milestone 3 (first invoice created → first invoice sent) — the single biggest drop-off on the activation funnel. Today's surfaces for this cohort are the on-page draft-send-banner (only visible on `/invoices/:id` itself) and the 24h stale-draft email/dashboard prompt; neither covers the same-day return-to-dashboard cohort, which is the exact moment when a freelancer who just created a draft is most likely to re-enter looking for "what do I do next?". Free, Pro, and Agency all see the same nudge — the cohort this attacks (drafted-but-never-sent within 24h) cuts across every plan. Every recovered first-send fires the `first-sent-celebration` email + flips the invoice into the client-viewed/sent-not-viewed/overdue/payment-claim/paid-receipt downstream cascade and into the activation-funnel "sent_one" stage that gates every Milestone 4 surface.
Master action: none — pure-code builder + dashboard view block + one new test file + one-line `package.json` test list addition + two-line wiring in the GET `/` render call. No schema migration, no new DB calls, no env vars, no new dependencies, no Stripe/Resend touch. Reuses the existing `/invoices/:id/status` POST route + CSRF middleware + the already-loaded user row + invoices list.

Shipped: 48h "pending payment-claim" follow-up email cron (`jobs/pending-payment-claim-followup.js` at 16:00 UTC, strictly after the other re-engagement crons at 09:00 / 11:00 / 12:00 / 13:00 / 14:00 / 15:00). Closes the relationship-degrading Milestone 4 gap that today's surfaces miss: when a CLIENT clicks "I've sent payment" on the public `/i/<token>` share page we already fire `sendPaymentClaimedEmail` to the freelancer immediately, but if the freelancer doesn't see that email (Resend outage, spam folder, simple inbox-miss) and doesn't return to the dashboard (where `payment_claim_prompt` fires), the claim just sits there — the client believes they paid, the freelancer hasn't acknowledged, and after 48h the client wonders "did they get my note?" and the trust window closes. cron wiring (NODE_ENV=test block, force-accept, double-start refusal, cron tick wires processPaymentClaimFollowup, `DEFAULT_SCHEDULE === '0 16 * * *'` strictly after sent-not-viewed at 15:00); SQL contract (status<>'paid', is_seed=false, payment_claimed_at IS NOT NULL, min-age uses $1, max-age uses $2, cooldown gate, welcome_email_sent_at IS NOT NULL, ORDER BY ASC, LIMIT 500, projection includes method+reference+note for email body, negative/non-numeric input sanitization); `db.markPaymentClaimFollowupSent` SQL contract (UPDATE shape, updated_at bump, IS NULL re-assertion in UPDATE for concurrent-tick safety, RETURNING shape, parameter binding, falsy-invoiceId UPDATE short-circuit). ## 2026-05-23
Shipped: client-facing "Paid: Invoice X — thank you" receipt email that fires the moment an invoice flips to paid via either the manual `POST /invoices/:id/status` Mark-as-Paid flow OR the Stripe Payment Link webhook (`checkout.session.completed` with `mode='payment'`). Closes the silent close-the-loop gap that existed for the client side of every paid invoice: before today the client could only learn about the paid state by revisiting the `/i/<token>` share page — and freelancers who marked paid manually (cash / cheque / Venmo / Zelle / payment-claim widget confirmation) had no way to send a receipt without composing one by hand. New `invoices.client_paid_receipt_sent_at TIMESTAMP` (idempotent ALTER) gates a one-shot stamp per invoice; `db.markClientPaidReceiptSent(invoiceId)` is a race-safe `UPDATE … WHERE id=$1 AND client_paid_receipt_sent_at IS NULL RETURNING id, client_paid_receipt_sent_at` so a Stripe-retry + manual-flip race collapses to exactly one stamping row. New `lib/email.sendPaidReceiptEmail(invoice, owner)` short-circuits with `invalid_args` on null arguments and `no_client_email` when the invoice has no recipient; subject is `Paid: Invoice <N> — thank you` (distinct from `sendPaidNotificationEmail`'s freelancer-facing "just paid" framing — this one is a formal confirmation, not a celebration), body is `Hi <client>, this is a confirmation that <sender> has marked invoice <N> for <$total> as paid. Thanks for the prompt settlement.` with HTML escape on both `client_name` and `senderName(owner)` (XSS defence even though the only renderer is the recipient's mail client). When the invoice has both `APP_URL` configured AND a `public_token`, a "View receipt" green button links to `https://<host>/i/<token>` — the same public share URL the client already has, now permanently rendering the paid status — but the email gracefully omits the button when either is unset rather than rendering a dead link. Reply-To follows the existing owner precedence (`reply_to_email → business_email → email`) so a client reply lands in the freelancer's mailbox, not Resend's. New `lib/paid-receipt.triggerPaidReceipt(db, invoice, owner)` orchestrates: guards on missing `db.markClientPaidReceiptSent`, `is_seed=true` (defence-in-depth — the dashboard sample is never a real receipt), empty/whitespace `client_email` (trimmed before send), and `client_paid_receipt_sent_at` already-set short-circuits without a DB call. Stamp-then-send ordering prevents double-emails under a Stripe webhook retry race + concurrent manual flip; on Resend `not_configured` the orchestrator un-stamps via a parameterised `UPDATE invoices SET client_paid_receipt_sent_at = NULL WHERE id = $1` so a future mark-paid flip retries cleanly once the key is provisioned (but on any other send failure the stamp is preserved — re-spamming the client on retry is worse than a single missed receipt). Wired into both paid-flip sites: `routes/invoices.js POST /:id/status` after the existing `triggerFirstPaidCelebration` (each is fire-and-forget so neither blocks the other or the redirect), and `routes/billing.js` Stripe webhook after the existing `triggerFirstPaidCelebration` inside the `session.mode === 'payment'` branch (subscription-mode upgrades never fire — they aren't invoice payments). 28 new assertions in `tests/paid-receipt-email.test.js` across six layers: formatters (subject framing distinct from "just paid"; HTML XSS escape on client_name + senderName; view button conditional on APP_URL + public_token; text body normalises trailing-slash APP_URL; "behalf of <sender>" disclaimer surfaces); sendPaidReceiptEmail (invalid_args on null args, no_client_email on missing email, happy-path recipient is the CLIENT not the freelancer with reply_to via owner precedence); triggerPaidReceipt (null args, missing-method, seed short-circuit, missing/empty/whitespace client_email, already-stamped short-circuit avoids DB call, stamp throw soft-fail, stamp race-lost no-op, happy path stamps + sends + trims, send throw preserves stamp, not_configured un-stamps with parameterised query); db.markClientPaidReceiptSent SQL contract (UPDATE shape, NOW() stamp, updated_at bump, IS NULL guard, RETURNING shape, parameter binding, falsy-id short-circuit before any SQL); route integration (status=paid fires trigger once; status=sent does NOT; status=paid with no client_email still 302s and the route still calls the trigger, leaving the skip-decision to the helper); webhook integration (payment_link mode fires once, subscription mode does NOT, trigger throw still returns 200 — fire-and-forget). Full suite: 100 test files, 0 failures.
Advances: Milestone 4 (first invoice sent → first payment received) — closes the client-facing half of the paid moment, building the trust and credibility that compound into repeat business (the `repeat-client-prompt` shipped earlier today is the freelancer-side surface that turns each paid moment into the next first-sent event; this is the client-side counterpart that makes the client want to come back). Today the receipt gap means a client who paid via Venmo or who tapped a Stripe pay-link has no inbox artefact confirming "yes, your payment landed and the invoice is now settled" — they have to chase the freelancer or guess. The Stripe Pay Link path's `sendPaidNotificationEmail` is freelancer-facing only; until today there was no client-facing equivalent on any path. Works across all plans (free + Pro + Agency — every paid-flip on any plan benefits) and across every paid mechanism (manual mark-paid from cash/cheque/Venmo/Zelle/bank transfer/PayPal, payment-claim widget confirmation, Stripe Pay Link checkout). The activation funnel impact is indirect but real: clients who receive professional paid receipts are dramatically more likely to recommend the freelancer (which compounds across the freelancer's referral loop) and to engage on the next invoice (which fires the repeat-client-prompt's `/duplicate` CTA on the freelancer side).
Master action: none — pure-code schema migration (one idempotent ALTER on invoices) + new db helper + new email helpers + new orchestrator lib + two route wiring sites + new test file + package.json test list addition. ## 2026-05-23
Shipped: dashboard "repeat-client" prompt that fires whenever the user has a recently-paid invoice (`status='paid'`, `updated_at` within the last 7 days, non-seed) AND no currently-open invoice (draft/sent/overdue) for that same client. Renders a green banner directly below the overdue-prompt slot ("X just paid — send the next invoice while you're top of mind") with a one-tap CTA that POSTs to the existing `/invoices/:id/duplicate` route (free-tier limit + cross-tenant guards already in place), landing the user on `/invoices/:newId/edit` on a fresh draft pre-filled from the source invoice's client + items. New pure helper `buildRepeatClientPrompt(invoices)` in `routes/invoices.js`: filters to non-seed real invoices, builds a case-insensitive trimmed Set of open-invoice client names, then scans for the most-recent `status='paid'` row whose normalized client name is NOT in that Set and whose `updated_at` falls inside `[now - 7d, now]` (future timestamps rejected as clock-skew defence; unparseable timestamps skipped). Returns `{ sourceId, invoiceNumber, clientName, total: Number, daysAgo }` or null. Threaded into the existing `GET /` handler alongside the other 9 prompts; the dashboard error-fallback render also seeds `repeatClientPrompt: null` so a Promise.all throw never leaves the template with `undefined`. View block carries `data-testid="repeat-client-prompt"`, `print:hidden`, CSRF-stamped form, daysAgo=1 singular grammar, daysAgo=0 "today" copy, and full EJS escape on the hostile client_name path. Why `updated_at` as the "paid recency" signal: the schema has no per-invoice paid_at column, but `updated_at` is bumped on every status change including sent→paid; the false-positive (paid 30d ago, edited 5d ago) is harmless because the prompt is non-destructive — the user reviews the duplicate before sending. Suppression on same-client open work is critical: we never prompt to invoice a client who already has work in flight, since that would race the user's current draft/overdue/sent flow; the case-insensitive trim also collapses spelling/spacing inconsistencies (`"Acme Corp"` and `"  ACME CORP  "` are the same client). Seed sample rows are filtered out entirely, so a seed draft for the same client name doesn't suppress (the seed never represents real work). When the most-recent paid invoice IS blocked by an open invoice for that client, the helper scans the older paid invoices and surfaces the next-oldest qualifying one — a freelancer with multiple paying clients sees ANY of them whose pipeline is empty. 36 new assertions in `tests/repeat-client-prompt.test.js`: helper (null on non-array/empty, seed filter, non-paid status filter, empty/whitespace client_name, missing id, missing/unparseable/future/old updated_at, happy-path shape, trim, stringy total, null total, daysAgo=0, inclusive 7d boundary, most-recent picker, open-draft/sent/overdue suppression, case-insensitive suppression, paid-paid no-suppress, different-client no-suppress, seed-open no-suppress, older-paid fallback when newer blocked, injected `now` for deterministic timing); view (omit on null, render on set, content surfaces, CTA form action/method/CSRF/testid, daysAgo=1 singular, daysAgo=0 "today" copy, XSS escape, print:hidden, positional ordering below overdue-prompt). Full suite: 99 test files, 0 failures.
Advances: Milestone 2 (first dashboard re-entry → first real invoice created) and Milestone 3 (first invoice created → first invoice sent) — every duplicate created from this prompt re-enters the full create→sent→paid funnel, multiplying the activation events per signup. The Primary Objective targets the SIGNUP→FIRST-SENT rate; this surface turns each paid moment into the next first-sent event for that client relationship while the trust is still hot. The biggest single-action repeat-business lever we can ship without a full recurring-invoices feature: a freelancer who just got paid is in the exact moment the next retainer / next phase / follow-up project converts highest, and today there is no in-app push to seize that moment — the celebration banner fires once per first-paid event ever, and no surface fires on subsequent payments. Works across all plans: free users gate at 3 real invoices (the duplicate route already enforces this), Pro/Agency get unlimited duplicates. Compounds with every downstream surface: each new draft fires the stale-draft prompt + email if not sent, the share-intent and auto-flip flows on send, the client-viewed/sent-not-viewed/overdue prompts post-send, and the first-paid celebration on payment.
Master action: none — pure-code helper + view block + test file + one-line wiring in the dashboard handler + package.json test list addition. No schema migration, no new env vars, no new dependencies, no Stripe/Resend touch. Re-uses the existing CSRF-protected `/invoices/:id/duplicate` POST route shipped earlier.

## 2026-05-22
Shipped: "📅 Add to calendar" .ics download on the public `/i/<token>` share page for sent/overdue invoices with a `due_date`. New `lib/calendar.js` builds an RFC 5545-compliant .ics body (BEGIN/END VCALENDAR + VEVENT, VERSION:2.0, PRODID, deterministic UID anchored on invoice id + public_token so re-imports update instead of duplicating, DTSTAMP in UTC, DTSTART/DTEND as VALUE=DATE with the exclusive-end-day convention for an all-day event, SUMMARY+DESCRIPTION naming invoice/sender/amount/URL, URL property pointing at the share page, STATUS:CONFIRMED, TRANSP:TRANSPARENT, and a VALARM with TRIGGER:-P0DT12H so the client's OS fires a "due tomorrow" notification around noon the day before). New `GET /i/<token>/calendar.ics` route in `routes/share.js` serves the body with `text/calendar; charset=utf-8`, `Content-Disposition: attachment; filename="<invoice-number>-due.ics"` (ASCII-safe filename with path-traversal and non-ASCII fallback), `Cache-Control: no-store` (so due-date edits on the freelancer side aren't masked by intermediary caches), and `X-Robots-Tag: noindex`. The route 404s on bad-format token (no DB call), unknown token, draft status (pre-share), paid status (nothing upcoming), and missing due_date — only sent/overdue invoices with a due_date serve the file. Lookup throw renders a 500, never a silent empty body. Public view (`views/invoice-public.ejs`) gets a new "📅 Add to calendar" anchor in the existing print bar, gated on `due_date && (status === 'sent' || status === 'overdue') && public_token`, positioned BEFORE the Save-as-PDF button (calendar is the action-bias surface, PDF is the archive surface), carries `download` + `aria-label="Add invoice due date to your calendar"`, and inherits the print bar's `print:hidden` so it drops out of the printed PDF. RFC 5545 line-folding (75 octets with single-space continuation) is applied defensively to handle long DESCRIPTION values; text fields are escaped per §3.3.11 (backslash, comma, semicolon, CRLF) so hostile invoice/owner names can't truncate the event or inject pseudo-properties. 35 new assertions in `tests/public-share-ics-download.test.js` across three layers: lib pure formatters (UTC date/datetime, RFC 5545 text escape including compound backslash-then-special-char, line-fold under/over 75 chars with continuation-space contract, filename safety incl. path traversal + non-ASCII fallback, null inputs); buildInvoiceIcs (envelope shape, DTSTART/DTEND span the all-day event correctly, DTSTAMP carries supplied `now`, SUMMARY+DESCRIPTION+URL+amount surface, deterministic UID across two calls, VALARM with 12h trigger, null on missing/garbage due_date, null on missing invoice, hostile-input escape, fallback sender label when all owner fields are blank); route (200 + every response header, 404 on bad-format/missing/paid/draft/no-due-date, 200 on overdue, 500 on db throw); view (link renders on sent + overdue, suppressed on paid/draft/no-due-date/no-public-token, print-bar print:hidden inheritance, DOM order before Save-as-PDF, regression guard that the existing PDF button + `window.print()` onclick are untouched). Full suite: 97 test files, 0 failures.
Advances: Milestone 4 (first invoice sent → first payment received) — a client with the due date pinned in their native calendar gets an OS-native day-before reminder regardless of whether they're back in email or signed into our app, and regardless of whether the freelancer is on Pro (the Pay-now CTA + reminders cron) or free (no Stripe + no automated client emails). The single most effective on-time-payment intervention we can ship without owning the client's notification surface, and the only Milestone 4 lever today that fires WITHOUT requiring a return-to-app event from either party. Compounds with every previously-shipped M4 surface: the public-share section's payment instructions, the Pay-now hero, the payment-claim widget, the overdue freelancer digest, the 48h client-viewed-followup, the 72h sent-not-viewed nudge, and the in-app dashboard prompts that mirror those crons.
Master action: none — pure-code lib + route + view + tests; no schema migration, no env vars (the existing `APP_URL` already gates absolute-URL generation in the .ics body, falling back gracefully to a relative form if unset), no Stripe/Resend dependency (download is fully client-initiated, served by our own host), no new npm dependencies.

NOT plan-gated — free-tier users especially benefit (a stale-WhatsApp-number share has zero recourse today). CTA: "Open invoice & re-share" baked with a 7-day magic-login URL (?next=/invoices/<id>) so a freelancer clicking the reminder hours later lands signed-in on the invoice page where the share-intent buttons live. Shipped: 48h+ "client viewed but hasn't paid" follow-up email cron (`jobs/client-viewed-followup.js` at 14:00 UTC, strictly after the overdue-digest at 13:00). Fills the gap between two existing surfaces: the real-time `sendClientViewedEmail` fires the INSTANT of first client open and stops; the `overdue-freelancer-digest` only fires AFTER `due_date` passes (typically weeks later). The 2-14 day post-view window between is the peak conversion moment — the client demonstrably saw the invoice and is now sitting on it — and was un-nudged today. New `invoices.client_viewed_followup_sent_at TIMESTAMP` (idempotent ALTER in `db/schema.sql`) + partial index on `(first_viewed_at)` filtered to `status IN ('sent','overdue') AND first_viewed_at IS NOT NULL AND client_viewed_followup_sent_at IS NULL` so the cron query is sub-millisecond even on a large invoices table. `db.getInvoicesForClientViewedFollowup(minHours=48, maxDays=14)` selects unpaid (status IN ('sent','overdue')) non-seed invoices whose `first_viewed_at` sits inside the 48h–14d window with `client_viewed_followup_sent_at IS NULL AND u.welcome_email_sent_at IS NOT NULL AND u.email IS NOT NULL`, ordered by `first_viewed_at ASC` (oldest-viewed first) and capped LIMIT 500 per tick. Stamping uses `db.markClientViewedFollowupSent(invoiceId)` — guarded `UPDATE … WHERE id=$1 AND client_viewed_followup_sent_at IS NULL` so concurrent ticks idempotently single-fire even under a race. Per-INVOICE stamp (not per-user) so a freelancer with multiple unpaid-viewed invoices each gets their own follow-up window. NOT plan-gated — free-tier users get the freelancer-side push to fire share-intent WhatsApp/SMS/Email follow-ups (their only automated nudge since the Pro client-reminder cron is plan-gated), Pro users get the same nudge (lift independent of tier). Email content: `<CLIENT> opened <INV> but hasn't paid yet — send a nudge?` subject, "they opened it N days ago" anchor, optional "they've opened it X times — clearly considering it" line when `view_count > 1`, "a short friendly nudge at this exact moment is the single highest-converting action" framing, primary CTA "Open invoice & send a follow-up" baked with `?next=/invoices/<id>` so the click auto-signs-in straight to the invoice page where the share-intent buttons + mark-as-paid live. Magic-login mint is per-row with 7-day TTL via `lib/magic-login.mintMagicLoginToken`, soft-fails to the plain `/invoices/<id>` URL on mint failure (warn logged), and a mint throw is a non-error soft-fail so the send still ships. `not_configured` (RESEND key unset) is a clean skip — no stamp written, next tick retries. Job wired into `server.js` startup alongside the other re-engagement crons (trial-nudge, stale-draft, no-invoice, second-no-invoice, overdue-digest). 28 new assertions in `tests/client-viewed-followup.test.js`: formatters (subject embeds client+invoice+"hasn't paid"+"nudge", graceful "your client opened your invoice" fallback on null fields, XSS escape on hostile name/client, day-since-viewed anchor, view_count>1 multi-open line + view_count=1 omission, APP_URL trim, no-CTA graceful degradation when APP_URL unset, "2 days" floor so subject/copy never says "0 days"); `daysSinceViewed` arithmetic (48h→2, 5d→5, null→0, garbage→0); orchestrator (happy path stamps by invoice_id, magic-login mint per-row with cross-user leak guard, mint failure → plain CTA fallback + stamp still lands, mint throw → soft-fail + email still ships, ttlMinutes opt override, `?next=` deep-link passes `safeNextPath`, replyTo precedence reply_to_email → business_email → email, no-email skip, `not_configured` no-stamp retry, mid-batch send throw, top-level query failure → errors=1); cron wiring (NODE_ENV=test block, force-accept, double-start refusal, cron tick wires processClientViewedFollowup, `DEFAULT_SCHEDULE === '0 14 * * *'`); SQL contract (status IN sent/overdue, is_seed=false, first_viewed_at IS NOT NULL, min-age uses $1, max-age uses $2, cooldown gate, welcome_email_sent_at IS NOT NULL ordering, ORDER BY ASC, LIMIT 500, negative/non-numeric input sanitization, falsy-invoiceId UPDATE short-circuit, NULL guard re-asserted in UPDATE for concurrent-tick safety). New `users.first_sent_at TIMESTAMP` (idempotent ALTER in `db/schema.sql`) gates a single one-shot stamp; `db.recordFirstSentIfMissing(userId)` is a race-safe UPDATE guarded on `first_sent_at IS NULL AND EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM invoices WHERE user_id=$1 AND status IN ('sent','paid','overdue') AND COALESCE(is_seed, false) = false)`, so concurrent flips arriving milliseconds apart on the same user's first invoice collapse to exactly one stamping RETURNING row — seed invoices are SQL-excluded so the dashboard sample marked-sent never fires the celebration. `lib/first-sent-celebration.triggerFirstSentCelebration(db, userId, invoice)` mirrors the `lib/celebration` first-paid pattern: idempotent stamp → 7-day magic-login mint → fire-and-forget Resend send. Email content: `<INV>` subject for inbox skimmability, congratulations greeting, "most freelancers get paid within 1–2 weeks" expectation-setting bullets, one-tap follow-up reminder pointer, primary CTA "Open invoice <INV>" baked with `?next=/invoices/<id>` so the click auto-signs-in straight to the just-sent invoice, and a free-tier-only "Try Pro free for 7 days" upsell block (Pro/Agency owners never see the upgrade pitch). Magic-login mint failures (db_unavailable / persist_failed / db_error) and Resend `not_configured` are both clean soft-fails — the email still ships with the plain `/invoices/<id>` path on mint failure, the stamp is preserved across `not_configured` retries. Trigger wired into all four flip sites in `routes/invoices.js` (POST `/:id/status` for status=sent, POST `/:id/share-intent` on flip, POST `/:id/email-client` on flip, POST `/quick` on `create_and_email` success) and into `routes/share.js` (GET `/i/<token>` when `invoice.status === 'draft' && row.status === 'sent'`, gated on `!invoice.is_seed && invoice.owner_id` as belt-and-braces). Each trigger call is fire-and-forget — never blocks the status-flip redirect or the public render. Reinforces Milestone 3's strongest upstream activation event before payment: the celebration email is the first transactional confirmation a brand-new user gets that the system actually delivered for them, which is exactly the moment to (a) set realistic payment-timeline expectations and (b) plant the Pay-Link upsell with a free-trial CTA for free users. 24 new assertions in `tests/first-sent-celebration.test.js`: SQL contract (UPDATE shape, IS NULL guard, EXISTS subquery on sent/paid/overdue + is_seed exclusion, null on no-op, falsy-userId short-circuit), email helpers (not_configured default, missing-args rejection, subject/HTML/text field embedding, free-tier upgrade block + Pro/Agency omission, magic-login URL bake + APP_URL fallback, hostile-input HTML escape), orchestrator (fresh-stamp happy path with 7-day mint, no-op when already stamped, DB throw → null, missing-args → null, mint soft-fail still ships email), route integration (status=sent fires trigger; status=paid does NOT; share-intent flip fires once; share-intent no-flip skips; email-client flip fires; share-view auto-flip fires for the owner; already-sent share view skips; seed invoice share view skips). Full suite: 86 test files, 0 failures.
Advances: Milestone 3 (first invoice created → first invoice sent)

## 2026-05-20
Shipped: server-side autosave + recovery for in-progress `/invoices/quick` drafts. New `pending_quick_invoice JSONB` + `pending_quick_invoice_updated_at TIMESTAMP` columns on `users` (idempotent ALTER) plus two new db helpers (`setPendingQuickInvoice`, `clearPendingQuickInvoice`). The quick-invoice Alpine scope now owns all four form fields under `fields.{client_name,client_email,description,amount}` and debounces a 800ms `@input` autosave to a new `POST /invoices/quick/autosave` endpoint, which normalizes the JSON body (strips unknown keys, clamps strings at 500 chars / amount at 32, coerces non-strings to empty/string-form) and writes to the column — or clears it if every field is empty after the user backspaces. `GET /invoices/quick` reads the column via the new `readPendingQuickInvoice` helper and pre-fills `submitted` so the form inputs come back populated on next visit; an amber "📋 Picked up where you left off" banner renders above the form when the prefill fires (suppressed when a validation flash is showing, so it never stacks with the re-render path). `POST /invoices/quick` best-effort calls `clearPendingQuickInvoice` after a successful create (try/catch so a failed clear never blocks the redirect — verified by an explicit test). Dashboard gets a new "📝 Pick up your draft invoice for <client>" banner just above the celebration banner / stale-draft prompt, built from a new `buildPendingQuickInvoiceBanner` helper that tolerates null user, null column, all-empty payloads (treated as no pending), stringified JSONB (legacy/test-stub shape), and non-string field types (coerced to empty). Banner copy adapts to what's populated: client name appears in the headline when set, description+amount in the subline; with only an amount typed, the banner still renders with just the amount and "Continue invoice →" CTA pointing back at `/invoices/quick`. 19 new assertions in `tests/quick-invoice-autosave.test.js` across four layers: route POST /autosave (5 — happy path normalizes to the 4-field shape, all-empty clears, unknown fields stripped, oversize fields clamped to the 500/32 limits, non-string types safely coerce); route GET /quick (3 — pending row pre-fills inputs + restored banner renders, null pending omits banner, all-empty pending row collapses to no-banner); route POST /quick (2 — clear-pending fires on successful create, clear-pending throw does not block the 302 redirect); dashboard render (3 — banner with all fields renders with the testid hooks, banner hidden when null, banner renders with just amount when client/description are empty); helper-level gate (6 — null user, null column, all-empty payload, stringified JSONB parse, non-string field types ignored, trimmed clientName/description/amount in the returned banner payload). Updated 1 assertion in `tests/invoice-quick.test.js` (the `:disabled="!clientEmail"` → `:disabled="!fields.client_email"` Alpine binding moved under the shared autosave scope). Full suite: 82 files, 0 failures.
Advances: Milestone 2 (first dashboard re-entry → first real invoice created). Today's `/invoices/quick` form has zero memory across sessions — a user who starts typing then bounces (phone rings, tab closes, they realize they need to fish out the client's email) loses every field and gets a blank form on return. The autosave + dashboard banner pair recovers that cohort: any keystroke gets persisted within 800ms, and on next dashboard visit a brand-coloured banner names what they were working on and one-clicks them back into the half-finished form. Critically, the dashboard banner is the re-engagement surface — without it, the user has to remember to navigate back to `/invoices/quick`, which they often don't. Compounds with every downstream activation/conversion surface (the stale-draft prompt, the first-real-invoice hero, the trial-urgency stack, the exit-intent modal, the celebration banner, the referral loop, the locked-feature upsells, the welcome-email magic-login CTA) because every recovered draft is one more user in the sent-invoice population the entire funnel depends on. Free, Pro, and Agency all see the same autosave — there's no plan gate; the cohort this attacks (typed-and-bounced) cuts across every plan and is the dominant Milestone-2 drop-off after the seed-invoice hero already shipped.
Master action: none — pure-code schema migration (two idempotent ALTERs) + two db helpers + one new route + two route modifications + one view modification + one dashboard banner block + one test file; no env vars, no new dependencies, no third-party API surface, no PII expansion (the JSONB column holds only the four form fields the user is already typing into a form). The schema migration is the only operator-visible side effect and is idempotent on every deploy (matches the same `ALTER TABLE … ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS` pattern every previous column addition has used).

The email button is Alpine-bound (`x-model="clientEmail"`) so it disables and re-labels to "Add client email to send ↓" when the email field is empty, then flips to "📧 Create & email to client →" when populated — no dead-end clicks. `POST /invoices/quick` reads `req.body.action`: when `create_and_email` AND `user.plan in ('pro','agency')` AND `client_email` is present, after `db.createInvoice` it calls `lib/email.sendInvoiceEmail(invoice, user)` and on `ok:true` fires `db.markInvoiceSentFromShareIntent` for the atomic draft → sent flip (same helper the share-intent + `/:id/email-client` paths already use, so the activation-funnel `sent_one` counter ticks identically). Advances: Milestone 3 (first invoice created → first invoice sent) compounding with Milestone 2 (first dashboard re-entry → first real invoice created). Today's two-step path is: user opens /invoices/quick → types 3 fields + email → submits → lands on /invoices/:id with banner + share buttons → reads which button to click → clicks "Send by email" → page reloads → status flips. After this ship: user opens /invoices/quick → types 3 fields + email → clicks "Create & email to client" → done, status=sent on the next page load. That's a click + a decision-beat removed from the activation path, and decision-beats are the dominant drop-off in mobile flows (the user has to read 4-5 different share buttons to pick the email one). Targets the highest-yield activation cohort: trial-Pro users (the only ones who get the button) who are exactly the cohort the downstream trial→paid conversion stack depends on. Every trial-Pro user who creates+sends in one click instead of bouncing at the "what now?" beat is one more user the celebration banner, exit-intent modal, referral hook, and locked-feature upsells get to fire on. Free users see no change to their surface — the dual-button only renders when the plan unlocks the value, and the existing pro-lock copy on /invoices/:id still owns the upsell motion.

The `RESEND_API_KEY` operator action that gates every transactional email covers this surface too — until set, the create-and-email branch sets the user-facing flash explaining that email delivery is not configured yet and points them at the share-intent buttons on /invoices/:id.

The explicit one-tap button collapses both paths into "click button → see ✓ Sent to acme@example.com → page reloads showing Sent status" — real delivery, real confirmation, real status flip. Compounds with every downstream conversion surface (trial-urgency stack, exit-intent modal, celebration banner, referral loop, locked-feature upsells, follow-up share intents) because every cohort user it activates lands them in the sent-invoice population that the rest of the funnel depends on. After: click welcome CTA → auto-sign-in → land on `/invoices/quick` → type three things → submit → land on `/invoices/:id` with prefetched share-intent buttons (native + WhatsApp/SMS/Email) one tap away from sent. ## 2026-05-19
Shipped: Native Web Share API "Share via…" button on `/invoices/:id` — adds a new `data-share="native"` entry to the existing share-intent surface (alongside WhatsApp/SMS/Email) that fires `navigator.share({ title, text, url })` so the freelancer opens their device's native share sheet instead of being locked into the three messaging targets we have explicit deep-links for. On iOS Safari + Android Chrome that share sheet exposes Telegram, Signal, Slack, Teams, Facebook Messenger, Instagram DM, Discord, Twitter/X, ProtonMail, AirDrop, Files.app, and every other "Open With" target the user has installed; on macOS Safari + Edge / Opera it lifts the OS share menu (Notes, Reminders, Mail, Messages, etc). Renders in both share scopes: the public-share-section (free + Pro, payload from the prefetched `intents.subject` / `intents.body` / `url`) and the Pro pay-link section (payload from the same `shareSubject` / `shareBody` / `invoice.payment_link_url` strings the WhatsApp/SMS/Email anchors already use, JSON-encoded into the Alpine `@click` so the share sheet target receives byte-identical copy). The button is `x-show`-gated on a new `nativeShareSupported` Alpine state that defaults to `false` and only flips `true` in `x-init` after `typeof navigator.share === 'function'` — so Firefox (no Web Share support) silently hides it instead of surfacing a dead-end button. Auto-flip parity: `'native'` is the fifth member of `SHARE_INTENT_KINDS` in `routes/invoices.js`, so the existing `@click="fireIntent('native'); nativeShare(...)"` runs the same atomic `db.markInvoiceSentFromShareIntent` flip that WhatsApp/SMS/Email/Copy already trigger — a freelancer who shares via Telegram still moves their dashboard from draft → sent and ticks the activation-funnel `sent_one` counter. The `nativeShare(data)` helper double-guards on `typeof navigator.share === 'function'` (defensive against extension-shim values) and wraps `navigator.share(data).catch(() => {})` so the inevitable AbortError when the user cancels the share sheet never surfaces as an unhandled rejection. `x-cloak` on the button avoids first-paint flash before Alpine boots. 10 new assertions in `tests/native-share-button.test.js` across two layers: route (intent='native' on draft → 200 + flipped=true + status=sent + correct mark call args; intent='native' on already-sent → 200 + flipped=false; unknown intents still rejected as 400 with no DB write) + view (button rendered in both share scopes with correct testids, `x-show`/`x-cloak` gating, `fireIntent('native')` + `nativeShare(...)` wiring, prefetched intents threaded through public-share-section's nativeShare payload, Stripe pay-link URL threaded through Pro pay-link section's payload, `nativeShareSupported: false` default + `navigator.share` detection in `x-init`, `.catch()` on the navigator.share promise, `typeof === 'function'` guard inside the helper). Full suite: 79 files, 0 failures.
Advances: Milestone 3 (first invoice created → first invoice sent) — the existing WhatsApp/SMS/Email share intents collapse the most common share paths but exclude the targets that dominate non-North-American freelancer markets and B2B agency comms. Telegram is the primary client-comms channel for huge cohorts in Europe / MENA / Asia / LATAM; Signal is the default for privacy-conscious clients; Slack / Teams is where B2B agency invoices actually land; Messenger / Instagram DM is where freelancer-to-small-business invoices flow; Discord is the creator-economy client channel; AirDrop closes the in-person handoff loop. Every one of those targets is a freelancer who today has to copy the share URL, switch apps, paste, type a greeting, and remember to come back and mark sent — a 5+ step flow vs. the 1-tap flow WhatsApp/SMS/Email get. Web Share API collapses all of them into one tap that ALSO fires the existing auto-flip so the dashboard tracks the send. Distinct from the other share-intent buttons because it's the only one that lets the freelancer pick the target (vs. choosing WhatsApp/SMS/Email up-front); a freelancer who normally uses Signal as their default messenger can now share invoices through it without us ever shipping a Signal deep-link. Plan-neutral — every cohort gets the lift on Milestone 3 throughput.
Master action: none — pure-code route + view + test change; uses only Web Platform APIs already on every shipping browser; no schema migration, no env vars, no Stripe/Resend dependency, no new npm packages.

## 2026-05-19
Shipped: 3-field "Quick invoice" express form at GET/POST `/invoices/quick` — a brand-new user creating their first real invoice now types client name + a one-line description + an amount (4 fields with the optional client_email) and lands on `/invoices/<id>` with share-intent buttons pre-rendered, instead of facing the 5+ blank fields the full `/invoices/new` form requires (line-items builder, qty, unit price, tax rate, notes, dates, address). The dashboard's "Create your first real invoice" hero CTA now points at `/invoices/quick`; a secondary "Need line items, tax or custom dates? Use the advanced form →" link (`data-testid="first-real-invoice-prompt-advanced"`) retains `/invoices/new` as an escape hatch for power users. Same `FREE_LIMIT=3` gate as POST /invoices/new (over-limit free users redirect to /invoices?limit_hit=1 on both GET and POST). Server fills in the rest: `invoice_number` from `db.getNextInvoiceNumber`, `issued_date = today`, `due_date = today + 30d`, `items = [{description, quantity: 1, unit_price: amount}]`, `subtotal = total = amount`, `tax_rate = tax_amount = 0`, `client_address = notes = null`, optional `client_email` (empty → null). Reuses the existing `db.createInvoice` helper which already bumps `users.invoice_count` and stamps `is_seed=false` by schema default, so a quick invoice slots into every downstream surface (firstRealInvoicePrompt gate, activation-funnel `created_real` stage, /invoices/:id share-intent prefetch + auto-flip-on-share-intent, stale-draft prompt + email cron, overdue cron, celebration banner, referral hook) identically to any other real invoice. Validation runs express-validator: `client_name` trimmed non-empty, `description` trimmed non-empty (the validation error reads "Describe what you did so the client recognises the bill" — soft copy that nudges the user toward a useful line description rather than a typo), `amount` `isFloat({ gt: 0 })` so zero/negative/missing/non-numeric all re-render with a "Amount must be greater than $0" flash. Validation re-render passes a `submitted` object through to the view so client_name / client_email / description / amount values stick across the form re-paint — the user fixes the one bad field instead of re-typing all four. New `views/invoice-quick.ejs` is a narrow 1-column form (`max-w-xl`) styled to match the existing form's input rings + dimensions; the amount input is dollar-prefixed and `step="0.01"`. Post-create flash sets `req.session.flash = {type:'success', message:'Invoice created — share it with your client below to mark it as sent.'}` so the user lands on `/invoices/<id>` with a green banner nudging them toward the prefetched WhatsApp/SMS/Email share-intent buttons — combined with today's earlier share-intent auto-flip ship, the entire path from blank dashboard to `status='sent'` is now four clicks (Create → 3 fields → Submit → share-tap), down from the previous 10+ click path through the full form. 18 new assertions in `tests/invoice-quick.test.js` across 4 layers: route GET (renders form for free below limit + Pro at any count, redirects free at limit to /invoices?limit_hit=1), route POST (happy-path createInvoice contract — single-item array with qty=1 + unit_price=amount, today's date + +30d due_date, invoice_number from getNextInvoiceNumber, server-side trim on client_name + description, empty client_email persisted as null not empty string, missing client_name / missing description / zero amount / negative amount / missing amount / free-tier-at-limit all gated correctly with zero createInvoice calls on every failure, sticky form repopulation on validation error), view rendering (form action + CSRF + the 3 required inputs + optional email + advanced-form escape hatch + flash gating + sticky-value repopulation), and dashboard integration (primary `data-testid="first-real-invoice-prompt-cta"` now href="/invoices/quick" + secondary `data-testid="first-real-invoice-prompt-advanced"` href="/invoices/new"). Updated `tests/first-real-invoice-prompt.test.js` to assert the new CTA pair (1 swap + 1 net-new line). The legacy empty-state fallback (the error-render path on `firstRealInvoicePrompt === null` and `invoices.length === 0`) was also migrated to the same primary-CTA-/quick + secondary-advanced-link-/new shape so the two surfaces stay consistent. Full suite: 78 files, 0 failures.
Advances: Milestone 2 (first dashboard re-entry → first real invoice created) — the blank-line-items form at /invoices/new is the largest documented Milestone 2 drop-off (the entire seed-invoice ship #39 + onboarding checklist + first-real-invoice hero + duplicate button stack exists to attack it). The Duplicate button (shipped earlier today) helps users who can use the seed as a starting point, but a freelancer whose work is nothing like the seed (different industry, different rate, different shape) still has to scrub the cloned content and re-fill every field. Quick is the lower bound on Milestone 2 friction: literally the three answers the freelancer needs to communicate to their client (who, what, how much). Every saved minute on this step compounds across every downstream conversion surface — the auto-flip on share-intent click and the prefetched WhatsApp/SMS/Email buttons mean the click-to-sent path is now closer to seconds than minutes for the cohort that gives up at the blank form. Plan-neutral (free / Pro / Agency all see the same form) so the activation funnel benefits across the entire cohort, not just the trial-Pro slice. Distinct from Duplicate because Duplicate requires a source to clone (seed or a past invoice); Quick is the cold-start path that needs nothing before it.
Master action: none — pure-code route + view + test change; reuses existing `db.createInvoice` / `db.getNextInvoiceNumber` / `db.getUserById`; no schema migration, no env vars, no Stripe/Resend dependency, no new dependencies. The CSRF + session + free-tier limit + validation patterns are the same ones every other authenticated POST in the app already uses.

## 2026-05-19
Shipped: welcome email's "Create your first invoice →" + "Start your free Pro trial" CTAs now carry a one-shot 7-day magic-login URL with `?next=/invoices/new` (and `?next=/billing/upgrade`) so a recipient who clicks days after signup lands auto-signed-in on the target page instead of bouncing at /auth/login. New `mintMagicLoginToken(db, userId, { ttlMinutes })` helper in `lib/magic-login.js` mints a magic-login token without the email-send side-effect that `requestMagicLink` carries; `lib/welcome.js#triggerWelcomeEmail` calls it after `markWelcomeEmailSent` lands the idempotency stamp and threads the URL into `sendWelcomeEmail(user, { magicLoginUrl })`. New `WELCOME_TTL_MINUTES = 7 days` (export) is wider than the 30-min DEFAULT_TTL_MINUTES used by user-initiated requestMagicLink — the welcome email is a triggered email that may sit unread for a weekend. Mint failures (db_unavailable / persist_failed / db_error / unexpected throw) soft-fail with a logged warning and `sendWelcomeEmail` is still invoked with the plain APP_URL paths — graceful degradation, the email always ships. `lib/email.js#welcomeUrls(opts)` now accepts `opts.magicLoginUrl`: when present, the two activation-CTA hrefs become `${magicLoginUrl}?next=/invoices/new` and `${magicLoginUrl}?next=/billing/upgrade`; when absent, falls back to the legacy `${APP_URL}/invoices/new` + `${APP_URL}/billing/upgrade`. `buildWelcomeHtml` / `buildWelcomeText` / `sendWelcomeEmail` all gained an optional `opts` 2nd arg; passing nothing preserves the pre-existing render exactly (backward-compat for any test or caller that didn't migrate). New `safeNextPath(raw)` helper in `lib/magic-login.js` validates `?next=` against a tight allow-list — `/dashboard`, `/invoices`, `/invoices/new`, `/billing/upgrade`, `/settings` — and rejects absolute URLs (`https://evil.com/...`), protocol-relative (`//evil.com`), schemes (`javascript:`, `data:`), control-char-laced strings (CRLF response-splitting payloads like `/dashboard%0d%0aLocation:%20https://evil.com`), empty/non-strings, and any path off the list. `routes/auth.js GET /auth/magic/:token` now reads `req.query.next` through `safeNextPath`, and the post-consume redirect lands at the validated next (or `/dashboard` as the fall-back) — every error path (bad shape / replay / expired / wrong-kind / db throw) still renders the unchanged "this link is no longer valid" 400 page. The route's pre-existing `redirectIfAuth` middleware is replaced with an inline check: an already-signed-in user clicking the welcome email CTA with `?next=/invoices/new` is forwarded to `/invoices/new` without consuming the still-valid token (so the same browser used to sign up doesn't burn the link), preserving the pre-existing /dashboard redirect when `next` is absent. 17 new assertions in `tests/welcome-magic-link.test.js` across 4 layers: pure-function safeNextPath (allow-list passes; absolute/protocol-relative/javascript/data URLs all rejected; off-list app paths like /admin/activation /auth/login / and /invoices/:id rejected; non-strings and empty strings rejected; CRLF/CR/LF/NUL/tab rejected before allow-list check), pure-function mintMagicLoginToken (db_unavailable on missing createPasswordResetToken; no_user on falsy id; happy path persists kind='login' + token = generateToken() + tokenHash = SHA-256(token) + ttlMinutes propagated + url = buildMagicLoginUrl(token); default TTL fallback; soft-fails reason='db_error' on createPasswordResetToken throw), lib/email + lib/welcome integration (buildWelcomeHtml with opts.magicLoginUrl embeds the magic URL on BOTH CTAs with the correct ?next= and STRIPS the plain APP_URL paths from the CTA hrefs; buildWelcomeText same in plaintext; absent opts falls back to legacy plain paths and contains NO /auth/magic/; triggerWelcomeEmail bakes the URL into the sent payload, calls mint exactly once with userId + ttlMinutes=WELCOME_TTL_MINUTES + kind=login, surfaces magicLoginUrl on the result; mint failure still ships the email with plain CTAs and surfaces magicLoginUrl=null), and route integration (?next=/invoices/new on happy path → 302 /invoices/new + session set; ?next=https://evil.com → 302 /dashboard + token still consumed; absent next → 302 /dashboard regression guard; authed user with ?next=/invoices/new → 302 /invoices/new WITHOUT consuming the token; CRLF-laced %0d%0a-in-next → 302 /dashboard + Location header free of CR/LF). Existing 15-assertion `tests/welcome-email.test.js` and 25-assertion `tests/magic-login.test.js` both still pass — the welcome tests exercise the no-opts path which falls back to the plain APP_URL hrefs they assert on, and the magic-login tests pass `?next=` absent which keeps the legacy /dashboard redirect intact. Full suite: 77 files, 0 failures.
Advances: Milestone 1 (signup → first dashboard re-entry) — the welcome email is the first re-entry surface every signup gets, and until today its CTA assumed the user was still signed-in on the device they clicked from. Mobile users who sign up on a phone, switch to laptop later, and open the welcome email there would hit /auth/login and bounce. Desktop users whose session expired (default Express session ~24h) would hit the same wall. With this ship, the welcome email's primary CTA carries an auto-sign-in token, so the click goes from email → /invoices/new (logged-in, ready to create), zero password decisions, zero login form. The 7-day TTL covers the weekend-reader cohort. Compounds with every downstream conversion surface — every freelancer who lands at /invoices/new from the welcome email is one create-action away from advancing to Milestone 3 (sent), Milestone 4 (paid), and the trial-urgency / celebration / referral surfaces that fire on real-cohort users.
Master action: none — pure-code lib + route + view-builder change; no schema migration (re-uses existing `password_resets` table + `kind='login'` column shipped with magic-login itself); no env vars; no new dependencies; no Stripe touch. The Resend `RESEND_API_KEY` operator action that gates magic-login emails covers this surface too — already tracked in MASTER_ACTIONS.

## 2026-05-19
Shipped: one-click "Duplicate" button on every owned invoice — `POST /invoices/:id/duplicate` clones the source row as a fresh draft (status='draft', is_seed=false, all bookkeeping stamps cleared) and redirects the user straight to /invoices/<newId>/edit so they can swap the client + items and save. Closes the blank-form drop-off at Milestone 2 (first dashboard re-entry → first real invoice created): a new user staring at the seed sample can now turn it into their first real invoice in one click, then customize, instead of staring at an empty /invoices/new form. Compounds with Milestone 3/4 (the repeat-client speed-up): a freelancer who landed one paid invoice can fire the next month's invoice for the same client in seconds. New `db.duplicateInvoice(sourceId, userId, { invoice_number, issued_date, due_date })` runs a single atomic INSERT…SELECT inside BEGIN/COMMIT — the SELECT projects the user-data columns (client_name/email/address, items, subtotal, tax_rate, tax_amount, total, notes) and the INSERT hardcodes `status='draft'` + `is_seed=false` so a duplicate of a seed or a paid invoice starts as a real, fresh draft. The owner gate is in the SELECT's `WHERE id=$1 AND user_id=$2`: cross-tenant ids return 0 rows, the transaction ROLLBACKs, and the function returns null without bumping invoice_count. Columns deliberately NOT named in the INSERT column list (and so reset to schema defaults) are the per-row lifecycle stamps: payment_link_url/_id (Stripe Pay Link is per-invoice), public_token (next share mints fresh), view_count + first_viewed_at + last_viewed_at (a duplicate is unviewed), sent_via_share_view_at + sent_via_share_intent_at (not sent yet), payment_claimed_at + payment_claim_method + payment_claim_reference + payment_claim_note (no pending claim), last_reminder_sent_at (no reminder history). The `UPDATE users SET invoice_count = invoice_count + 1` runs in the same transaction, so a partial failure (insert succeeds, count update throws) never strands the user with an un-counted real invoice. Falsy sourceId/userId/invoice_number short-circuits return null with zero DB calls. `POST /invoices/:id/duplicate` in `routes/invoices.js` enforces the free-tier `invoice_count >= 3` limit (redirects to `/invoices?limit_hit=1` exactly like POST /invoices/new), loads the source via `db.getInvoiceById` (the owner gate at the route layer — cross-tenant ids never reach the duplicate call), generates the next invoice_number via `db.getNextInvoiceNumber`, computes today's issued_date + a +30d due_date, calls `db.duplicateInvoice`, bumps `req.session.user.invoice_count`, flashes "Duplicated as a new draft — update the client and items, then mark it sent.", and 302s to /invoices/<newId>/edit. Source-not-found and a null duplicate result both gracefully redirect to /dashboard. `views/invoice-view.ejs` adds a "🗐 Duplicate" button in the existing action bar (next to Edit + Delete) wrapped in a CSRF-protected POST form (`data-testid="invoice-duplicate-form"` + `data-testid="invoice-duplicate-button"`); the button surfaces on every status (draft/sent/paid/overdue) and every plan (free/pro/agency). Seed-source invoices get an "as draft" copy suffix on the button label + a longer tooltip explaining the clone will be a real invoice (not another seed). 11 new assertions in `tests/invoice-duplicate.test.js` across 3 layers: db (INSERT…SELECT SQL contract with hardcoded `'draft'` + `false` + WHERE-id-and-user_id owner gate, RETURNING *, no public_token / view_* / payment_link_* / payment_claim_* / sent_via_share_* / last_reminder_sent_at in the column list — all reset to schema defaults; BEGIN/COMMIT on hit + ROLLBACK on miss with no invoice_count bump; pg client released exactly once; falsy-arg short-circuit), route (pro happy path → 302 to /edit with the next invoice_number + today's issued_date + +30d due_date computed correctly; cross-tenant → /dashboard with no duplicate call; free-tier at limit → /invoices?limit_hit=1 with no duplicate call; free-tier on seed-only → 302 /edit with the dup confirmed non-seed + draft + items copied verbatim + notes copied; source not found → /dashboard with no duplicate call), and view (button + form + CSRF + action URL on all 4 statuses for pro user; button surfaces on free-tier seed with "as draft" copy hint; non-seed invoice omits the "as draft" suffix). Full suite: 75 files, 0 failures.
Advances: Milestone 2 (first dashboard re-entry → first real invoice created) — the empty-state CTAs and onboarding checklist (both shipped) push the user toward `/invoices/new`, but `/invoices/new` itself is a blank-form wall: client + email + address + 4 line-item columns + tax-rate + notes + dates is a lot of typing to do without a reference. The seed sample exists specifically so the user has a finished invoice to look at, but until today the only way to use it was as a passive read-only reference. With this ship, the seed becomes an actionable starting point: the user clicks "Duplicate", lands on a pre-populated edit form with the seed's items + tax + notes already filled in, and just swaps "Sample Client (edit this)" → their real client. The cost of typing the first real invoice from scratch drops dramatically, especially for the cohort that gives up at the blank-form moment. Compounds beyond Milestone 2: every subsequent repeat-client invoice (the most common real freelance pattern — same client, new month or new project) is now one-click-fast, which raises the average invoices-per-user over a 30-day cohort window, which fattens the trial-urgency / celebration / referral / overdue-cron surfaces shipped earlier.
Master action: none — pure-code db helper + route + view + test; no schema migration (uses existing columns + existing schema-default fall-through for the cleared stamps), no env vars, no Stripe/Resend dependency, no new dependencies.
